## Q3. AI Foundation Quarterly Delta — deep research

```
**SAME-DAY DOUBLE-RUN GUARD (completeness-critic N-4, 2026-07-16 — see the shared Observability section's generalized guard)** — FIRST, before anything else: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='Q3' AND run_date=<today, America/Denver> AND status='completed'`; if `>= 1`, output "Q3 already completed today (a prior fire — OPS0 catch-up or Q3's own scheduled trigger — already completed; this is the expected redundant re-invocation, not an error)." and END IMMEDIATELY — do NOT re-run the delta, do NOT log another `started`/`completed` row.

Read access scope: Quarterly cadence. Query all of `events.decision_log` (no live/archive split, §15) — prior-cycle Q3 outcomes and AI-foundation-change dispositions are queryable there.

Read AI_Trading_Foundation.md (Parts 1, 2, 3, 4 in full — pay particular attention to the Tier framework distinction between architectural / structural Tier 1 items vs empirical / measured Tier 2 items, and to Part 4 verification protocol), Strategy.md, Experiment_Parameters.md, Operating_Protocols.md (positions from `state.current_positions`, regime from `state.current_regime`, decisions from `events.decision_log`).

**MODEL OF RECORD + EVIDENCE HIERARCHY — establish this FIRST, before any capability research (owner directive 2026-07-28). Self-contained here; A1 carries the same rule at annual depth.**

The in-use model is whichever model the OWNER configured on the remote-routine triggers — read `ops/cadence.yaml`'s top-level **`routine_model`** key, corroborate against `OWNER_ACTIONS.md` and (if the `RemoteTrigger` connector is available) a `get` on a trigger id from `ops/trigger_ids.json`. It is **NOT** the newest or most capable model released, and must **NOT** be inferred from this session's own self-reported identity. All remote routines always run the same model, so there is one answer. This is the value the Part 4 version-change protocol writes into the in-use-Claude-version field. If the key is missing or disagrees with a live read, say so and flag it rather than silently picking one.

Then gather Section 1 / Section 3a evidence at **four widening levels, reporting ALL FOUR** — because research on the exact deployed model is usually gated or too slow to exist yet, and counting only deployed-model evidence would discard nearly everything:
- **L1** — the deployed model exactly. **L2** — the same model line, any version (classify by capability class, not literal name). **L3** — any Anthropic/Claude model. **L4** — non-Anthropic models and autoregressive-LLM-general claims.
- **Do not confuse L1–L4 with the foundation's Tier 1 / Tier 2**, which classify items as architectural-vs-empirical. Orthogonal axes.
- **The weight ordering applies to MAGNITUDES only.** For Tier 1 architectural/existence claims, L4 generality evidence is the appropriate and often strongest class — do not discount it for being off-model.
- Give each item a **cross-level verdict** (CONVERGENT / LEVEL-SPLIT / VERSION-VOLATILE / SPARSE) and a one-line transfer assessment. VERSION-VOLATILE within L2 means a neighbouring version's number does **not** transfer to the deployed model regardless of how much L3/L4 evidence exists.
- The hierarchy **refines weighting and reporting; it is NOT a gate and adds no bar of its own, including for reductions.** §5.5 guardrail 2 is satisfied by Claude replication (L1/L2/L3) *or* an architectural-generality argument (L4) — so an L3-only or L4-only reduction clears it as the foundation currently writes it, and §5.5's own closing rule exempts direct research findings from the guardrails entirely. **Do not impose a stricter standard than §5.5 codifies.** For a reduction, REPORT the level and cross-level verdict alongside the §5.5 guardrail results so the downstream §5.3/§5.6 machinery can see what a relaxation rests on; if you judge the bar too permissive, emit a PART 2 proposal to amend **§5.5 guardrail 2** (not §5.4 — that is a magnitude-threshold table with no provenance condition) rather than applying a tighter rule on this run.
- This quarterly slice supersedes the legacy "transferability filter" wording elsewhere in this routine where the two disagree: classify evidence as L1–L4 and apply §5.5 as written; do not run two parallel transferability schemes.
- Claude-specific evidence is **not on Hugging Face** — use Anthropic system cards, `anthropic.com`, `platform.claude.com` deprecation pages, and independent third-party evaluators. A general paper whose panel happens to include a Claude model is L2/L3 evidence; check panels rather than assuming L4.

Frame research adversarially: look for evidence that contradicts or updates documented edges and disadvantages, not evidence that confirms them. Offsets self-reference bias (this task evaluates LLM claims while being executed by an LLM).

HF tool orientation. Read `HF_Resource_Catalog.md` once at session start for the authoritative HF tooling map. Apply the following operational rules during PART 1 research:
- **The `paper_search` MCP tool NO LONGER EXISTS** (verified 2026-07-28) — use `hf_fs` instead: `cmd="search"`, `args=["hf://papers", "<query>", "--limit", N]`, with `cmd="cat"`, `args=["hf://papers/<arxiv_id>/paper.md"]` for detail. Run each of the 9 query batteries enumerated in `HF_Resource_Catalog.md` §6.1 through `hf_fs`, scoped to every calendar quarter since Q3's last successful completion (normally just the prior quarter). For each battery, record any new papers from the prior quarter that bear on documented disadvantages or surface new failure modes — record arXiv IDs in the form `hf.co/papers/<id>`.
- For Section 3a benchmark results, iterate `hub_repo_search` over open-weights model authors (`meta-llama`, `Qwen`, `mistralai`, `deepseek-ai`, others) with `repo_types=["model"]` and read recent (prior-quarter) model cards for benchmark trajectory updates on benchmarks mapped per `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §5.5 (cross-reference the inverse mapping in `HF_Resource_Catalog.md` §2).
- Do NOT use `space_search` for canonical Spaces — per `HF_Resource_Catalog.md` §8.1, semantic search misses popular Spaces. Use `hub_repo_search` with explicit `author` and `repo_types=["space"]` for known Space lookup.
- HF is silent on Anthropic/Claude. Section 1 (Claude model capability changes) is `web_search` / `web_fetch` only — query `anthropic.com`, `docs.anthropic.com`, Anthropic research blog, and Anthropic-tagged news. Do NOT attempt to find Claude data on HF.
- HF Spaces and HF datasets are **not ingestible through the current connector set** — dataset contents need Python execution the routines do not have (`HF_Resource_Catalog.md` §4), and no financial Space is durable enough to embed as a multi-year routine dependency (§3). So HF's *usable* surface here is papers, model cards, and leaderboards-as-references. This is a capability fact, not a ban on the underlying evidence: if a Space or dataset carries a genuinely decision-relevant measurement, cite the underlying paper, model card, or primary site instead — and if the connector set later gains the ability to read them, use it. Per the shared "evidence lists are floors, not ceilings" rule, nothing here bounds what this routine may consult off-HF.
- Combine HF and `web_search` / `Tavily` deliberately: HF for academic LLM research and open-weights model evidence; web_search for vendor announcements, regulatory developments, and analyst-bias literature (per `HF_Resource_Catalog.md` §1.11, HF is weak on the classical accounting/finance literature).
- Query `[HF Frontier-LLM Capture]` entries from `events.decision_log` covering every calendar quarter since Q3's last successful completion (normally just the prior quarter; all history queryable, no live/archive split). These are mid-quarter material findings that D1's light-touch daily HF check captured but did not act on. Treat them as required priors when running the §6.1 query batteries — verify each captured paper is incorporated into the relevant section, and check whether any has been superseded by newer work in the prior quarter.

Produce AI capabilities research for every calendar quarter since Q3's last successful completion (normally the single prior quarter / 3 calendar months; if one or more quarterly cycles were missed, cover each missed quarter, oldest first, plus the immediately-prior quarter — do not silently skip a quarter's capability releases; owner directive 2026-07-25 CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW, see Observability § above). Write the complete content directly to `Quarterly_AI_Foundation_Delta.md` (overwrite; first line = prior calendar quarter in YYYY-QN format).

Scope. Section 1 is scoped to Anthropic/Claude only — the workflow's decision-maker is Claude, so capability releases from other providers do not change what this workflow can do. Sections 2–6 stay broad because they describe the reference class (autonomous-AI trading performance baseline), the evidence base for architectural failure modes (which replicate more robustly when observed across model families), or the environment (market structure and regulation) regardless of which model the workflow uses. Non-Claude evidence in Sections 2, 3, 5 is filtered for transferability in PART 2 before triggering foundation-change assessment.

PART 1 — Coverage of every calendar quarter since Q3's last successful completion (normally just the prior quarter; label each covered quarter explicitly). Primary sources only (papers, arXiv preprints, company announcements, regulatory filings, reputable news citing primary sources). If a section is empty, state so.

Section 1 — Claude model capability changes
New releases, version updates, deprecations from Anthropic (Claude family only). Per item: name and version, release date, Anthropic capability claims (reasoning, tool use, long-context synthesis, calibration, math), independent benchmark results. Include deprecation notices and scheduled end-of-life dates for any Claude version currently in use or plausibly in use within the next review cycle. When this session covers 2+ quarters (time-sensitive section): label each finding with which covered quarter it's from, and check whether a later covered quarter's release supersedes an earlier one's deprecation notice before reporting it as current.

Section 2 — AI trading performance research and reported results
New papers, arXiv preprints, hedge fund or prop firm disclosures, live-capital LLM trading arena updates, brokerage reports. Per item: methodology, models evaluated, results. Flag any item that evaluated a Claude model specifically.

Section 3 — LLM failure mode and bias research
New research on documented disadvantages and any newly-identified failure modes. Include replications. Per item: models evaluated. Flag items that evaluated a Claude model specifically or that claim architectural generality.

**Section 3a — Benchmark results bearing on Tier 2 disadvantages.** For each Tier 2 disadvantage with documented benchmark mapping per AI_Trading_Foundation.md §5.5 (the table in that section maps disadvantages to primary benchmarks), report any new benchmark results in the prior quarter that bear on the disadvantage. Per result: benchmark name and version, models evaluated, score and direction of change vs prior measurements, citation. Flag whether the result clears §5.5 Goodhart guardrails (≥3 sources, transferability, sustained, domain coverage) — single-source results are reported but flagged as not yet sufficient for reduction confirmation.

Section 4 — Market saturation and AI-driven market structure
AI adoption, retail AI use, AI share of trading volume, capital concentration through specific foundation models. Synchronized AI-driven market events. Regulator reports.

Section 5 — Adversarial content and manipulation risks
Prompt injection research, adversarial content targeting AI consumption, AI-generated content in financial documents. Per item: models evaluated where relevant.

Section 6 — Regulatory developments affecting AI in financial decision-making
New regulations, consultations, enforcement actions affecting AI-driven trading, robo-advisory, algorithmic decision-making.

Six sections, reverse chronological within each, citations inline.

PART 2 — Verification answers and per-strategy effects. The downstream Q4 routine reads this PART 2 verbatim and schedules per-strategy foundation-change assessment events per YES verdicts, so make YES/NO answers structured and per-strategy effects explicit. **WRITE-ONCE (owner directive 2026-07-25 CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW) — backed by a real, queryable pre-insert check, not prose discipline alone (hardened 2026-07-25, mirroring Q1's PART 2 WRITE-ONCE fix):** when this session's window covers 2+ quarters, answer each verification question below ONCE, aggregating evidence across all covered quarters — never once per quarter — so Q4 never receives duplicate/conflicting PENDING_ANALYSIS entries for the same strategy from a single catch-up session. The `state.strategy_candidates` and `entry_type='strategy-retirement-signal'` writes in the STRATEGY-ARSENAL BRANCHES below carry the same mechanical enforcement as Q1's: the candidates-table IDEMPOTENCY check (A3's `source_routine`-scoped pattern) and the `source_session`-keyed pre-insert check on retirement-signal rows, with `source_session` = this run's own `<session_id>` on every such write (never a per-quarter "`Q3 <date>`"-style label) — see SL4's corroboration test, which depends on this convention to be enforceable.

Answer each AI_Trading_Foundation.md Part 4 verification question explicitly using PART 1 as evidence. Default bias: YES — err toward flagging change, err toward triggering per-strategy foundation-change assessment. The experiment accepts false-positive assessments to catch real changes.

Transferability filter for non-Claude evidence. For PART 1 findings drawn from models other than Claude (Sections 2, 3, 5), a finding triggers per-strategy foundation-change assessment only if at least one of the following is present: (a) replication on a Claude model, (b) architectural generality — the mechanism is a documented property of autoregressive LLMs broadly rather than a specific model's training artifact, or (c) evidence from an Anthropic-family model (prior Claude version). Non-Claude findings lacking transferability evidence are logged in the YES/NO answer and flagged as watch items for the next review cycle, but do not trigger foundation-change assessment on their own.

Verification questions (per AI_Trading_Foundation.md Part 4):
Q1 — Has any AI capability in Part 1 materially changed? (Tier 1 by structural change, or Tier 2 by measurement update.)
Q2 — Has any AI disadvantage in Part 2 been reduced or eliminated by capability changes? (Triggers constraint-relaxation review per Experiment_Parameters.md if reduction is material AND the strategy has constraints flowing from the reduced disadvantage.)
Q3 — Has any new disadvantage emerged that is not listed?
Q4 — Have any Part 3a questions been resolved by accumulated evidence?
Q5 — Has market saturation changed in a way that shifts edge accessibility?
Q6 — Has a synchronized-AI market event occurred in the prior quarter?
Q7 — Have calibration records confirmed or refuted any edge or disadvantage claim?
Q8 — Has new research surfaced specific new failure modes or confirmed edges?

Note: rev 3 dropped the previous Q8 ("model deprecation or version update") from triggering refresh — version changes follow a separate protocol per AI_Trading_Foundation.md Part 4 §"Version-change protocol" (no early refresh; flip Tier 2 to version-pending; quarterly delta picks up version-specific research as it emerges).

Per YES: (a) evidence triggering the yes, (b) whether the evidence clears the transferability filter (if non-Claude), (c) which Tier the affected item is (Tier 1 architectural / Tier 2 magnitude), (d) strategies affected, (e) which foundation-change-assessment branch is warranted (continue / terminate / constraint-relaxation per Experiment_Parameters.md §Foundation change trigger).
STRATEGY-ARSENAL BRANCHES (rev 2026-07-10 — Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive). Beyond the per-strategy continue / terminate / constraint-relaxation effects above, emit two machine-readable arsenal branches in PART 2's actionable-outputs table, consumed autonomously (no "recommendation for A3 / participant" dead-end):
- **strategy-adoption.** If a capability change, a newly-actionable edge, or a materially-changed foundation implies a NEW or restart strategy archetype (especially one covering a zero/under-covered `state.arsenal_regime_coverage` cell), write a structured `state.strategy_candidates` row (`source_routine='Q3'`, `status='NEW'`, `candidate_code` = next free provisional code not present in `state.strategy_candidates` or `state.strategy_roster` — A3's collision-free rule; never reuse or guess a code) with the cited edges/disadvantages and target regime cells — SL1 qualifies it (default-REJECT) next quarter. **IDEMPOTENCY (owner directive 2026-07-25 CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW, mirroring A3's own `source_routine='A1'` seed check verbatim):** before inserting, skip any archetype that already has a `source_routine='Q3'` row with `status IN ('NEW','QUALIFYING')`.
- **strategy-retirement.** If the evidence is a sustained edge-decay short of a mechanical foundation-change terminate, surface it as an SL4-consumable signal (recorded in the actionable-outputs table + `events.decision_log`), written via `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)` with `entry_type='strategy-retirement-signal'` (same structured `fields` shape as Q1: strategy_code + signal_class + evidence), `in_source_session` = this run's own `<session_id>` (per PART 2's WRITE-ONCE bullet above — NOT a per-quarter "`Q3 <date>`" label), rather than a participant recommendation; SL4 evaluates it monthly against `state.strategy_retirement_candidacy` (default-KEEP, affirmative RETIRE required). **IDEMPOTENCY:** before this CALL, `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM events.decision_log WHERE entry_type='strategy-retirement-signal' AND strategy=<code> AND source_session=<this run's session_id>`; skip the CALL if `>= 1`.
Version-field updates continue to self-apply or route to A3 as today.

OUTPUT: write the complete content (PART 1 + PART 2) directly to `Quarterly_AI_Foundation_Delta.md`. First line is the YYYY-QN marker. Chat output: one-line acknowledgment.
```

## Q4. Quarterly Action Conversion — regular routine

Runs after Q2 and Q3 are saved. Q1 has no actionable outputs and does not gate Q4.

```
Read access scope: Quarterly cadence. Query `events.decision_log` for any cross-references needed (all history queryable, no live/archive split, §15). Read `Strategy.md`, `Experiment_Parameters.md`, `AI_Trading_Foundation.md`, `Operating_Protocols.md`, `Watchlist.md` as relevant (positions from `state.current_positions`, regime from `state.current_regime`, decisions from `events.decision_log`). Read `ops/cadence.yaml`, `ops/triggers.json`, and `ops/trigger_ids.json` for the quarterly trigger audit (step E).

**DEPENDENCY GATE — SEPARATE and FATAL, do NOT wrap, do NOT substitute the TRADING-ENABLE gate below for this — `CALL ops.sp_assert_deps('Q4', ['Q2', 'Q3'], <today>)` BEFORE the start-log, before anything else in this routine.** Aborts (RAISE) + raises a `missing_dependency` critical if Q2 or Q3 have no `completed` run anywhere in the current quarterly period — Q2/Q3/Q4 are `quarterly_ftd`, so since `bigquery/114_period_aware_dependency_gate.sql` (2026-07-29) the gate is satisfied by any Q2/Q3 completion with `run_date BETWEEN DATE_TRUNC(in_run_date, QUARTER) AND in_run_date`, not only a completion on this exact calendar day; self-bootstrapping. This is the general rule from Observability § above, restated here because it is the one call in this routine that must never be skipped (a 2026-07-09 audit found an analogous D2 session skip this exact call for its own dependency, so it is now inlined per-routine rather than left to a shared preamble alone).

**TRADING-ENABLE GATE — A STAGING GATE, NOT A ROUTINE GATE (self-improvement audit B-1-obs, 2026-07-03; blast-radius fix 2026-07-27, `INCIDENT[ref=423ecc02-fdb7-4f47-9445-d8c79d399e8c]`).** `CALL ops.sp_auto_resolve_alerts()` (best-effort), then read the gate NON-FATALLY: `SELECT trading_enabled, halt_reason FROM state.trading_enabled`. If FALSE, `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert_once('critical', 'Q4', 'trading_halted', 'Order staging blocked: trading is HALTED. See payload for the triggering routine and reason.', TO_JSON_STRING(STRUCT('Q4' AS routine, <halt_reason> AS halt_reason)))`, record the verdict, and **CONTINUE** — do NOT abort. Q4 crafts NO orders at all: its entire body is `PENDING_ANALYSIS` enqueue and Watchlist.md work that D2 later drains, and D2 re-checks the gate at its own craft points. So a FALSE verdict blocks NOTHING here — it is recorded for the audit trail and the routine completes normally. Aborting instead would discard a full quarter's research into a queue that was never written, to prevent order staging this routine does not do. Should Q4 ever gain a direct order-craft step, that step — and only that step — must re-check the verdict and decline per D2's staged-but-halted pattern.

Read the just-saved quarterly research files:
- `Quarterly_D_Candidates.md` (Q2 — D candidate shortlist with readiness flags)
- `Quarterly_AI_Foundation_Delta.md` (Q3 — YES/NO verification answers with foundation-change-assessment branches per strategy)

Convert into operator-actionable outputs per the operating model. Claude resolves all decisions internally; commissions disregarded at staging time.

A. D THESIS CONSTRUCTION FROM Q2 — for the Q2 ranked shortlist:
   - For each candidate marked "ready now": enqueue a `PENDING_ANALYSIS` entry (`INSERT INTO events.queue_events`; analysis_type thesis-construction; strategy D; due_date today; context from Q2 + Strategy.md D criteria + Operating_Protocols.md; conservative_default decline), ordered by thesis-strength rating. No cap. D2 drains them.
   - For each candidate marked "deferred pending rally pause": add to Watchlist.md D-deferred section with the trailing-30-day momentum reading and resolution-trigger ("when 30-day trailing return drops below X%"), AND enqueue a `PENDING_ANALYSIS` entry (`INSERT INTO events.queue_events`; analysis_type re-screen; strategy D; due_date = 30 days out; context = the re-check condition; conservative_default skip) so D2 re-checks then.
   - For each candidate marked "blocked by concentration": add to Watchlist.md D-blocked section with the specific blocker and resolution condition ("when GICS <sector> concentration < 30%"). No queue entry — this resolves when an existing D position closes (M4 D-exit handling triggers re-evaluation). (Correlation bucket is informational only, Rev 35, owner directive — it is never itself a blocker, so it cannot produce a "blocked by correlation bucket" candidate; D also has no position-count ceiling.)

B. FOUNDATION-CHANGE ASSESSMENT FROM Q3 — for each YES verdict that cleared the transferability filter and warrants foundation-change-assessment:
   - Per the strategies-affected list in the Q3 entry, enqueue a `PENDING_ANALYSIS` entry (`INSERT INTO events.queue_events`) per affected strategy (analysis_type foundation-change-assessment; strategy; due_date today; context = Q3 evidence summary + affected Tier (1 architectural / 2 magnitude) + the branch warranted (continue / terminate / constraint-relaxation) + reference to Experiment_Parameters.md §Foundation change trigger procedure; conservative_default = no change / continue). D2 drains them.
   - For NO verdicts and YES verdicts that fail the transferability filter: no action; logged as watch items, reviewed at next Q3 cycle.
   - **FOUNDATION-CHANGE RE-REVIEW CHECKLIST (finding STRUCTURAL, 2026-07-17 — the root pattern behind the two 2026-07-17 CRITICALs: the SGOV→VOO park cutover silently invalidated SGOV-era calibrated constants with nothing forcing a re-review).** Whenever a foundation change is being ACTIONED — the **park vehicle**, the **benchmark**, the **model version**, or the **regime vocabulary** changes (whether surfaced by a Q3 foundation-change verdict here, executed via the Operating_Protocols.md §13 park-policy cutover, or an A3 `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` edit) — mechanically run **`ops/foundation_change_review.md`**: it enumerates which calibrated constants / vocabularies / bands must be re-validated for each change class (e.g. a park-vehicle change re-validates the -15% soft / -40% hard book-drawdown breaker, the D2a ±15% NAV connector-sanity band, and the benchmark/park-beta assumptions). Complete each applicable line and write an `events.decision_log` entry (`CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)`, `entry_type='foundation-change-review'`) recording the change, which constants were re-validated, and any re-calibration performed. In-band and mechanical — NO new human gate (it does not route to review or await a confirm-tap); it is a checklist-completion requirement on the routine actioning the change, not an approval step.
   - **MODEL-OF-RECORD SYNC — QUARTERLY BACKSTOP (cadence audit 2026-07-29).** **Runs every quarter regardless of Q3's verdicts** — same as its sibling FOUNDATION-CHANGE RE-REVIEW CHECKLIST bullet immediately above, which carries the identical disclaimer: despite sitting inside this section B's "for each YES verdict" framing, a model-of-record change is a deployment fact wholly unrelated to whether Q3 produced any foundation-change YES verdict this cycle, so this check-then-act runs unconditionally every quarter, not only alongside a YES verdict. **This step also sits behind Q4's own FATAL `sp_assert_deps('Q4', ['Q2', 'Q3'])` gate (Dependency gate, above):** on a firing where Q2/Q3 have not completed anywhere in the current quarterly period, Q4 aborts before ever reaching this step, so the backstop simply does not run that quarter — D3's daily sync and A3's annual backstop remain the safety net. Read `ops/cadence.yaml`'s top-level `routine_model` key (corroborate against `OWNER_ACTIONS.md`; a live `RemoteTrigger` read wins over both if available), then read the in-use-Claude-version field at `AI_Trading_Foundation.md`'s document head and its Part 4 restatement — the two Tier-M sites per Part 4's "Write authority — two tiers" subsection. **If both document sites already equal `routine_model`: do nothing** — no edit, no revision bump, no decision-log entry, no alert. Normally this IS a no-op: D3 runs this same check daily, so by the time Q4 reaches it the field is already current. This bullet exists as the BACKSTOP for the stretch where D3 was missed or its own Tier-M edit failed to land — see D3's **MODEL-OF-RECORD DOC SYNC** step. **If either document site differs from `routine_model`** (including the two document sites merely disagreeing with each other — itself drift to repair, not a case to leave alone): **LAST-MOMENT RE-CHECK first, same as D3's step (shrinks the concurrent-writer collision window — D3/Q4/A3 sessions can overlap, and there is no `merge=union` driver for `.md` files) —** immediately before editing, not merely at session start, perform the same **NON-DESTRUCTIVE** re-check D3's step uses: `git fetch origin`, then read the field at both sites via `git show origin/main:AI_Trading_Foundation.md` — read that output only. **Never** `git reset --hard` or otherwise touch the working copy to do this: a Tier-M writer may be holding unrelated uncommitted edits to this same file, and A3 in particular always is (see D3's step for why). If D3 (or A3) already synced it in the interim, NO-OP here too: no edit, no revision bump, no decision-log entry, no alert. Otherwise, immediately following that re-check, perform the identical Tier-M write D3's step performs: update the field at BOTH sites, recording sourcing; flip ALL Tier-2-tagged numerical claims to version-pending-replication UNCONDITIONALLY, with no carve-outs, in the SAME edit (Part 4 step 4). **Scope the flip by the header-bracket tag ONLY** — the literal `[Tier 2` in an item's `### N.N Title [...]` header — **NEVER** by scanning the body for existing "VERSION-PENDING" text; **replace ONLY the blockquote paragraph beginning `> **VERSION-PENDING REPLICATION**`**, never any other paragraph in the same block — this rule is structural, not enumerative: items currently carrying such a second blockquote paragraph — A1-authored **Tier-J** fade-review content — include **1.3, 1.7, 2.4, 2.14, 2.15, and 2.19**; leave that second paragraph untouched on these items and on any other item that carries one, and **item 2.21 is `[Tier 1]`-tagged only** but carries an unrelated `> **VERSION-PENDING (figures only)**` citation-integrity blockquote that must NOT be touched by this flip (the worked example of why the header tag, not a text scan, governs — Part 4's write-authority subsection). Run `ops/foundation_change_review.md` §C "Model-version change" and write its completion record via `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)` with `entry_type='foundation-change-review'`; increment `AI_Trading_Foundation.md`'s revision number and append a revision-history entry; `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('info','Q4','model_of_record_synced', '<old value> -> <new value> — in-use-Claude-version field synced from ops/cadence.yaml routine_model (quarterly backstop — D3's daily sync missed this transition); new_value is read directly from routine_model and old_value is the field's value immediately before this write. Committed and pushed on this session''s branch; landing on main is subject to auto-merge and is NOT asserted here as already live — if this branch hits an auto-merge conflict, an auto-merge-conflict PR is opened and D3''s next daily run re-detects the still-stale field and re-syncs it, so the loss self-heals within a day for that reason, not because landing is guaranteed.', '<JSON: old_value, new_value, source="ops/cadence.yaml routine_model">')`. **Tier-J boundary unchanged:** Q4 must NOT touch item text, dispositions, new items, or `Strategy.md` — that stays A3-only.

C. WATCHLIST UPDATES — apply D-deferred / D-blocked additions from A.

D. DECISION-LOG ENTRIES — write a Q4-cycle outcome entry to `events.decision_log` (`CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)`) summarizing: Q2 candidates scheduled vs deferred vs blocked counts, Q3 foundation-change-assessment events scheduled per strategy.

E. QUARTERLY WEB-UI TRIGGER AUDIT (self-improvement audit ITEM 25, 2026-07-11; REWRITTEN 2026-07-16 — trigger-config-drift audit, ARCH-2/CC-5 merge: RemoteTrigger-first with autonomous self-heal). `ops/cadence.yaml`'s own header calls the web-UI cron trigger config "unversioned, invisible, un-reviewable, a single point of failure" — the 2026-07-10 audit that caught Q1-Q4/SL1/A1-A3 firing ~6h early (an MT wall-clock hour entered but evaluated as raw UTC) was a one-off manual pass, not a scheduled check. The prior version of this step dead-ended twice: a Chrome-reachable branch that assigned the live fix to the owner, and a headless branch whose `trigger_audit_needs_chrome` info alert was never emailed. `RemoteTrigger` get/update + `ops/trigger_ids.json` have existed and been headless-proven since 2026-07-12 (`ops/RUNBOOK.md` §41) — this step now audits AND self-corrects through them, each routine's structured `expected_trigger` in `ops/cadence.yaml` replacing the old free-text comment block as the enforced source of truth:
   - **PRIMARY (headless — no Chrome needed):** for each routine id in `ops/trigger_ids.json`, `RemoteTrigger get(<trigger_id>)`. Diff (a) the live schedule against that routine's `expected_trigger` in `ops/cadence.yaml` — for `recurrence: custom_cron` routines compare the cron string verbatim (a `cron_utc: TO_POPULATE` placeholder is populated from THIS observed value and committed: first-run bootstrap, not drift, no alert); for `recurrence: daily`/`weekly` routines convert `expected_trigger.time_local` to UTC using the America/Denver offset in effect TODAY before comparing (a mismatch fully explained by the MST/MDT hour is NOT drift); (b) the live instruction text against `ops/triggers.json`'s canonical instruction for that routine (whitespace-normalize both sides — collapse runs of whitespace, trim ends — before declaring drift); (c) enabled status (compare only — never auto-flip).
     - INSTRUCTION drift (any cohort) → SELF-CORRECT: `RemoteTrigger update(<trigger_id>)` with a body containing ONLY the instruction field set to the `ops/triggers.json` canonical string (never include schedule fields in this call), then `RemoteTrigger get` again to confirm the round-trip. `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('info','Q4','trigger_drift_corrected','<routine> — instruction drifted — restored from ops/triggers.json via RemoteTrigger update','<JSON: routine, field:"instruction", live_value, canonical_value>')` plus an `events.decision_log` entry (`entry_type='trigger-audit'`, per-routine diff summary).
     - SCHEDULE drift on a `custom_cron` routine → guarded self-correct (the 2026-07-10 incident this audit exists for WAS a schedule-encoding bug, so never construct a schedule body from scratch): (1) save the full `get` response as the rollback snapshot in the decision_log payload; (2) `RemoteTrigger update` with a partial body that reuses the get-response's own schedule-object shape with ONLY the drifted value corrected to `expected_trigger.cron_utc`; (3) `RemoteTrigger get` again and re-verify the schedule now matches the canonical cron AND the trigger is still enabled with the same recurrence type; (4) if the verify fails or the update errors, immediately re-apply the saved snapshot via a second update, and `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('warning','Q4','trigger_drift','<routine> — schedule drifted (live <x> vs canonical <y>) — self-correct round-trip failed, live config left as-found','<JSON: routine, field:"schedule", live_value, canonical_value, snapshot>')`. On success, raise the same info `trigger_drift_corrected` alert + decision_log entry as the instruction case; if `cron_utc` was still `TO_POPULATE`, commit the newly-observed value into `ops/cadence.yaml` (bootstrap, not drift).
     - SCHEDULE drift on a `daily`/`weekly` routine → NEVER `RemoteTrigger update` the schedule (these are native Daily/Weekly-picker triggers; `RemoteTrigger get` renders them as UTC crons while the live trigger is DST-aware — a raw cron write risks demoting one to a fixed-UTC Custom trigger, recreating the 2026-07-10 incident class). Instead `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('warning','Q4','trigger_drift','<routine> — schedule drifted (live <x> vs canonical time_local <y>) — NEVER auto-corrected on a daily/weekly trigger; paste this schedule + instruction into the web-UI trigger manually','<JSON: routine, field:"schedule", live_value, canonical_value>')`, with the exact canonical schedule + instruction in the message so the fix is a single owner paste.
     - A routine with an `expected_trigger` but ABSENT from `ops/trigger_ids.json`, a `get` that errors on a recorded id (deleted/recreated trigger), or a disabled trigger: `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('warning','Q4','trigger_missing_live','<routine> — no live trigger reachable; recreate with schedule <expected_trigger> + instruction <ops/triggers.json text>, then record its trig_ id in ops/trigger_ids.json','<JSON>')` — NEVER auto-enable or auto-create a trigger from this step.
     - NEVER call `RemoteTrigger run` from this step — re-firing missed routines is exclusively OPS0's job (its readiness view enforces the order-staging exclusion list; step E has no such rail).
   - **FALLBACK 1 (only if `RemoteTrigger get` errors for ALL ids this session — a tool outage, not per-id staleness):** the prior Claude-in-Chrome read-and-diff branch — read each trigger's schedule + Name/Instructions field verbatim in the console, diff the same way against `expected_trigger` / `ops/triggers.json`, and for any drift found `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('warning','Q4','trigger_drift', '<routine — what drifted and by how much>', '<JSON>')`, noting in the decision_log entry that the next session with RemoteTrigger access must apply the self-correct procedure above — not left to the owner.
   - **FALLBACK 2 (both surfaces unavailable this session):** `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('warning','Q4','trigger_audit_needs_chrome', 'Quarterly web-UI trigger audit could not run this cycle (no RemoteTrigger or Chrome/console access) — retry from a session with one of those available before the next quarter.', '<JSON: quarter, last_audit_date_from_cadence_yaml>')` — now WARNING (emailed); the old info-severity version of this alert was never emailed and is retired. **DO NOT word this alert as a request to re-apply `allowed_tools` or to "re-diagnose the config" (rev 2026-07-27).** That remediation is verified futile: `OWNER_ACTIONS.md` item U applied `RemoteTrigger` to all 30 then-existing triggers on 2026-07-19 with live echo-verification (commit `5a39d38`), and its own same-day addendum plus `ops/RUNBOOK.md` §15b record that headless/scheduled sessions still lack the tool **at the platform level regardless of what any trigger's `allowed_tools` lists**. An alert that asks the owner to re-apply a verified-applied, verified-insufficient config sends them on an errand that cannot succeed, every single time it fires. State the actual position instead: the sweep could not run because this is a scheduled session; the config is correct and is not the cause; the only real fix is platform-level (`RemoteTrigger` exposed to headless sessions, or a server-side trigger-audit API); and the working interim path is a manual sweep from an INTERACTIVE session, which does retain the tool. **PARTIAL-SWEEP NOTE (2026-07-27):** an interactive session's `mcp__Claude_Code_Remote__list_triggers` returns id / name / `cron_expression` / `enabled` / `ended_reason` / `suspension_reason` / `next_run_at`, which is enough to audit SCHEDULE drift, missing-live triggers, and silently-disabled triggers — the drift classes that actually cost a missed run. It does NOT return the instruction text or `allowed_tools`, so those two classes still require the full `RemoteTrigger get`. Prefer the partial sweep over no sweep, and say in the alert which classes were and were not covered. The same wording rule and the same partial-sweep option apply to OPS0 STEP 3, which mirrors this ladder verbatim.
   - Any branch: log the outcome (audited-via-api / audited-via-chrome / needs-chrome) to `events.decision_log` alongside the D. entry above, with checked/corrected/warned counts.

DEFERRAL DISCIPLINE: deferrals don't chain. Conservative-default fallback per deferred decision.

CALENDAR MCP USAGE: Q4 creates no calendar events — its analyses are enqueued to the `PENDING_ANALYSIS` queue (`events.queue_events`) and run by D2. Anchor dated due_dates on the America/Denver date. Time zone per Experiment_Parameters.md.

CHAT OUTPUT:
- No exit orders are produced by Q4 (D exits flow through M4; A/B/C/E exits flow through D2/W4).
- One-line acknowledgment of file edits and `PENDING_ANALYSIS` queue entries enqueued.

If no file changes, no queue entries: "No actions required."
```
