# ANNUAL (first trading day of January, or experiment anniversary month if January-anchoring isn't operationally clean)

A1, A2 are deep-research routines; A3 (action conversion) runs after A1 and A2 are saved.

## A1. AI Foundation Annual Full Re-Derivation — deep research

```
**SAME-DAY DOUBLE-RUN GUARD (completeness-critic N-4, 2026-07-16 — see the shared Observability section's generalized guard)** — FIRST, before anything else: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='A1' AND run_date=<today, America/Denver> AND status='completed'`; if `>= 1`, output "A1 already completed today (a prior fire — OPS0 catch-up or A1's own scheduled trigger — already completed; this is the expected redundant re-invocation, not an error)." and END IMMEDIATELY — do NOT re-run the re-derivation, do NOT log another `started`/`completed` row.

Read access scope: Annual cadence. Read everything, including full `events.decision_log` history (queryable, no live/archive split, §15).

Read AI_Trading_Foundation.md (Parts 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 in full — pay particular attention to the Tier framework distinction and to §5.4/§5.5), Experiment_Parameters.md, Operating_Protocols.md, the CURRENT `Quarterly_AI_Foundation_Delta.md`, and the prior `Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md` if one exists (positions from `state.current_positions`, regime from `state.current_regime`, decisions from `events.decision_log`). **Do NOT read `Strategy.md` whole** — it is ~366 KB and would consume most of the context budget before research starts; per the slice-map above, load `strategy/01_shared_regime_vocabulary.md` + `strategy/08_pre_mortems.md` (the pre-mortems carry the per-strategy foundation-citation graph PART 2 needs) and pull an individual `strategy/0N_strategy_<x>.md` slice only if a specific per-strategy question requires it. **Note the quarterly delta is a single overwritten file** — there is no series of "prior year's outputs" to read; earlier cycles' findings live in `events.hf_capability_captures` and `events.decision_log`, which are queryable and are the correct source for prior-cycle context.

**STEP A — MODEL OF RECORD. Establish this FIRST, before any capability research (owner directive 2026-07-28).** The foundation document describes the capabilities and limitations of **the model this experiment actually runs**, which is whichever model the OWNER configured on the remote-routine triggers — NOT the newest or most capable model Anthropic has released. Those are routinely different, and conflating them would make the entire document describe a model the experiment does not use. **All remote routines always run the SAME model** (standing owner invariant), so there is one answer, not one per routine.

Read `ops/cadence.yaml`'s top-level **`routine_model`** key — that is the version-controlled record of the owner's choice. Corroborate against `OWNER_ACTIONS.md` and, if the `RemoteTrigger` connector is available in-session, a `get` on any routine's trigger id from `ops/trigger_ids.json` (the live trigger config is ground truth; `routine_model` is a mirror of it and is the thing that is stale if they disagree — say so explicitly and flag it for the owner rather than silently preferring either). Do **NOT** infer the in-use model from this session's own self-reported identity alone, and do **NOT** assume it is the current frontier model. If `routine_model` is absent or unreadable, say so plainly, fall back to the corroborating sources, and state the assumption you proceeded under rather than guessing a version. Also report any of the four known hardcoded model-id sites that disagree with it: `ops/cadence.yaml` `routine_model`, the OPS2 trigger comment in the same file, `OWNER_ACTIONS.md`'s OPS2 model line, and OPS2's NO MODEL DOWNGRADE rule.

**STEP B — THE FOUR-LEVEL EVIDENCE HIERARCHY. Traverse ALL FOUR levels for EVERY item, EVERY run (owner directive 2026-07-28 — supersedes the earlier "non-deployed research is context only" rule, which was too blunt).**

*Why this exists.* Research on the exact deployed model will almost never be current: vendor evals are gated or self-reported, independent replication takes months, and by the time it lands the fleet may have moved on. A rule that only counts deployed-model evidence would therefore discard nearly everything and leave the foundation evidenced by almost nothing. Instead, gather evidence at four widening scopes and report all four, so the picture is complete and the *degree of transfer* is an explicit, reasoned judgement rather than an accident of what happened to be published.

**NAMING — do not confuse these with the foundation's own Tier framework.** `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` uses **Tier 1 / Tier 2** for *architectural-vs-empirical* item classification. The levels below are **L1–L4** and describe *how close the evidence is to the deployed model*. The two axes are orthogonal: a Tier 1 item can have L4 evidence, a Tier 2 item can have L1 evidence. Never write "Tier" when you mean a level.

- **L1 — DEPLOYED MODEL.** Measurements on exactly the `routine_model` id. Highest weight for magnitudes. Expect this to be sparse or empty, especially within a few months of a model switch — that is normal and is not a failure of the sweep.
- **L2 — MODEL LINE.** Same line as the deployed model, any version, version numbers disregarded (e.g. every Opus release when Opus is deployed). **Decision rule, applied in order, so this is reproducible run-to-run:** (1) same product-line NAME as the deployed model → same line; (2) different name but the vendor documents it as the direct successor/rename of that line → same line, cite the vendor statement; (3) otherwise → **different line, so it is L3, not L2.** Default to (3) — a wrongly-widened L2 is worse than a narrow one, because L2 is what licenses transfer. Concretely: with `claude-opus-5` deployed, other Opus releases are L2 while Sonnet, Haiku, Fable and Mythos releases are **L3** unless the vendor documents a rename. If the deployed model is the FIRST release of its line, L2 contains one member, line stability is unassessable, and every version-volatility question resolves to "cannot assess" — say that explicitly rather than defaulting to CONVERGENT.
- **L3 — ANTHROPIC / CLAUDE FAMILY.** Any Claude model across all lines. Captures vendor-level effects — the post-training pipeline, Constitutional-AI/RLHF variant, tokenizer, safety scaffolding — that apply across lines but not necessarily to other labs.
- **L4 — GENERAL / ARCHITECTURAL.** Non-Anthropic models, and claims asserted for autoregressive LLMs trained with current methods broadly. Widest scope.

**L4 IS NOT "WEAKEST" — the weight ordering applies to MAGNITUDES ONLY.** For a Tier 1 architectural or existence claim (autoregressive models lack internal arithmetic units; pre-training embeds historical outcomes; RL post-training degrades calibration), an L4 architectural-generality result is the *appropriate and often strongest* evidence class, because the claim is about the model class, not about one release. Do NOT downgrade a Tier 1 item's evidence merely because it sits at L4. Conversely, an L1 measurement of a *magnitude* outranks an L4 one for that magnitude.

**CROSS-LEVEL VERDICT — the analytical payoff. For each item, after gathering all four levels, assign exactly one. THE DEFAULT IS `SPARSE`; assign a more informative verdict only when its stated evidence bar is met, and on ambiguity record the MORE RESTRICTIVE verdict** (this mirrors the asymmetric defaults used everywhere else in `Experiment_Parameters.md` — tie goes to the conservative branch):
- **CONVERGENT** — levels agree in direction and magnitudes sit in a common band. **Bar: ≥2 independent sources, and where L2 is invoked, ≥2 distinct versions measured on the same dimension.** Non-L1 evidence transfers to the deployed model with high confidence.
- **LEVEL-SPLIT** — L1/L2 disagree with L3/L4. Something is specific to this model or line; trust the NARROWER level for **magnitudes** and say why. **For a Tier 1 architectural/existence claim, do NOT apply narrower-wins** — L4 generality remains the appropriate class there, per the magnitudes-only rule above.
- **VERSION-VOLATILE** — within L2, measurements swing materially across versions of the same line. **Bar: ≥2 versions measured on the same dimension, differing by enough to change a §5.4 band or reverse a direction.** This is the decisive negative case: a measurement on a neighbouring version is NOT good evidence about the deployed model, no matter how much L3/L4 evidence exists — that magnitude is version-pending regardless. (Worked example: an Opus-line long-context retrieval score that fell ~2.4× between consecutive releases is VERSION-VOLATILE; an ECE holding ~0.12 across three Claude models is CONVERGENT.)
- **OFF-LINE-ONLY** — **this is the EXPECTED MODAL CASE, especially in the months after a model switch: L1 and L2 are empty, but L3 and/or L4 carry real evidence.** It is emphatically NOT `SPARSE` — the evidence exists, it simply sits away from the deployed line. Transfer consequence: for a Tier 1 architectural/existence claim, L4 evidence is sufficient and the item resolves normally. For a Tier 2 **magnitude**, the number may be reported and carried, but it is a family/architectural estimate rather than a measurement of the deployed model — say so at the item, and treat it as version-pending if the in-use model changed this cycle.
- **SPARSE** — too little at **every** level to classify. Reserve this for genuine evidentiary emptiness; do not use it when L4 is rich (that is OFF-LINE-ONLY). Say so plainly; do not manufacture a verdict.

**TRANSFER ASSESSMENT.** State explicitly, per item, whether non-L1 evidence transfers to the deployed model and on what basis — normally the L2 volatility read. This replaces the blunt context-only rule: evidence is *weighted and reasoned about*, never discarded for being off-model.

**RELATIONSHIP TO §5.5 — the hierarchy REFINES reporting and weighting; it is NOT a gate, and it adds no bar of its own anywhere, including for reductions.** `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` §5.5 guardrail 2 (transferability) is satisfied by *either* Claude-family replication *or* an architectural-generality argument: **L1/L2/L3 satisfy the first, L4 satisfies the second, and an L3-only or L4-only reduction therefore CLEARS guardrail 2 as the foundation currently writes it.** §5.5's remaining guardrails (≥3 independent sources, sustained across ≥2 quarters or a 2-year window, domain coverage) do the conservative work, and §5.5's closing rule — direct research findings count regardless of benchmark coverage — is **not** subject to the guardrails at all. **A1 must NOT impose a stricter standard than §5.5 codifies** (e.g. "only L1/L2 counts for a reduction"): a routine instruction is not the place to change the foundation's criteria, in either direction.

**What A1 does instead for a reduction claim: REPORT the level, and PROPOSE if it disagrees.** State plainly which level the reduction evidence sits at and what the cross-level verdict is, so A3 and the downstream §5.3/§5.6 machinery can see exactly what a constraint relaxation would rest on. If the sweep judges that §5.5's transferability bar is too permissive for a capital-affecting relaxation — e.g. that an L4-only reduction on non-Anthropic models should not be able to loosen a sizing cap — then emit an explicit **PART 2 proposal to amend §5.5 guardrail 2**, with the reasoning and the proposed wording, for A3 to apply once it lands in the document. Do not apply the stricter bar on this run as though it were already the rule. (Note the correct cross-reference throughout: the transferability question lives in **§5.5 guardrail 2**, not §5.4 — §5.4 is a PARTIAL/MATERIAL magnitude-threshold table containing no evidence-provenance condition, so there is nothing in §5.4 for a provenance argument to clear.)

This is a FULL RE-DERIVATION task, not a delta. The scope is the LONGER of last-24-months primary-source research or the full period since A1's last successful completion (owner directive 2026-07-25 CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW, see Observability § above — in the ordinary case these are the same ~24 months, since A1 fires annually; only widen further in the pathological case A1 has been down for more than 2 years), not last-quarter. The purpose is to catch cumulative slow drift on Tier 2 numerical claims that quarterly deltas can't reliably surface, and to verify Tier 1 architectural items against any affirmative architectural-change evidence over a longer window.

**HOW TO RUN THIS AFFORDABLY — search by battery, then CLASSIFY into levels. Do NOT run a separate four-way search per item.** The research protocol is battery-shaped (9 topic queries) while the output is item×level-shaped; the bridge is a single classification pass, not repeated searching. Sequence: (1) run the batteries and anchor checks ONCE, building a pooled evidence set; (2) for every result, record the models actually evaluated by name and version, and assign it a level; (3) attribute pooled findings to items and emit the per-item level blocks from the classified pool; (4) only then run targeted gap-filling searches for items where a *decision-relevant* level is empty — typically L1/L2 on a Tier 2 magnitude. A naive per-item four-way search does not fit A1's slot and its overrun is not benign: A3 is `catchup_safe: false` and gated same-day on A1 and A2, so an A1 that runs long means `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` goes un-updated for a **year**, not a day.

**USE SUBAGENT FAN-OUT — this is expected, not optional.** Decompose the batteries across parallel sub-tasks (the prior sweep used eleven and it worked), each returning only its structured evidence block rather than raw transcript. Keep the orchestrating context for classification, cross-level verdicts and PART 2 synthesis. Per the shared Observability rules, run this routine's own guard, run-logging and model-of-record step in the orchestrating context FIRST — never delegate those to a sub-task.

**WHERE EACH LEVEL'S EVIDENCE ACTUALLY LIVES.** The levels need different sources, and searching the wrong surface for a level is the main way a sweep wrongly concludes `ABSENT`:
- **L1 / L2 / L3 (Claude-specific) — NOT on Hugging Face.** HF hosts no Anthropic model cards, evals, or announcements. Use `web_search` / `web_fetch` / Tavily against: Anthropic **system cards** (the richest L1 source — they carry per-surface injection, honesty and long-context tables, and are `VENDOR-CLAIMED` by definition), `anthropic.com/news`, `docs.anthropic.com`, `platform.claude.com` model-deprecation pages (for the release/retirement trajectory that defines the L2 line), and Anthropic's research blog. For **independent** Claude measurements — which are what L1/L2/L3 most need and most lack — check third-party evaluators and leaderboards that publish per-model numbers (e.g. Artificial Analysis and its indices, METR time-horizon reports, independent red-team firms, academic papers that happen to include a Claude model in their panel). When an academic paper's panel includes a Claude model, that paper is L2/L3 evidence *even if the paper is not about Claude* — check panels explicitly rather than assuming a general paper is L4.
- **L4 (general / architectural) — Hugging Face is the strong surface.** Use `hf_fs` for the paper index and `hub_repo_search` for model cards and leaderboards.
- Record for every measurement which model and version it was taken on. **A paper that names no model version cannot be placed at L1 or L2** — classify it L3 or L4 and say the version was unspecified rather than assuming.

HF tool orientation. Read `HF_Resource_Catalog.md` once at session start for the authoritative HF tooling map, but note the catalog is itself subject to drift — verify a tool exists before relying on it and report any correction needed. Apply the following operational rules during PART 1 research:
- **The `paper_search` MCP tool NO LONGER EXISTS** (verified 2026-07-28). Its replacement is `hf_fs`: search with `cmd="search"`, `args=["hf://papers", "<query>", "--limit", N]`; read detail with `cmd="cat"`, `args=["hf://papers/<arxiv_id>/paper.md"]` or `metadata.json`. Run each of the 9 query batteries enumerated in `HF_Resource_Catalog.md` §6.1 (Tier 1 architectural failure modes; cross-cutting; trading/financial) through `hf_fs`. For each battery, record the 3–5 most-cited / most-recent papers (last 24 months) with arXiv IDs in the form `hf.co/papers/<id>`. HF's paper index over-indexes vision-language work on hallucination queries — add "language model" / "text" / "agent" to disambiguate.
- Cross-check the durable anchors listed in §6.1 (StockBench `2510.02209`, FinanceBench `PatronusAI/financebench`, ReasonBENCH `2512.07795`, Beacon `2510.16727`, SynAnchors `2505.15392`, WAInjectBench `2510.01354`, BeliefShift `2603.23848`, Open LLM Leaderboard `open-llm-leaderboard/open_llm_leaderboard`) — verify each still exists and capture any successor work cited from them.
- For Tier 2 benchmark trajectories in §5.5, iterate `hub_repo_search` over open-weights model authors (`meta-llama`, `Qwen`, `mistralai`, `deepseek-ai`, others as relevant) with `repo_types=["model"]` and read benchmark scores from model cards. For benchmarks released or revised in the last 24 months that map to documented disadvantage categories (per §6.1 inverse mapping), capture the new measurements.
- Do NOT use `space_search` for canonical Spaces — per `HF_Resource_Catalog.md` §8.1, semantic search misses popular Spaces. Use `hub_repo_search` with explicit `author` and `repo_types=["space"]` for known Space lookup.
- HF is silent on Anthropic/Claude. For Section 1 Claude capability claims and any vendor-claim portion of other sections, use `web_search` and `web_fetch` against `anthropic.com`, `docs.anthropic.com`, and Anthropic's research blog. Do NOT attempt to find Claude data on HF.
- HF Spaces and HF datasets are **not ingestible through the current connector set** — dataset contents need Python execution the routines do not have (`HF_Resource_Catalog.md` §4), and no financial Space is durable enough to embed as a multi-year experiment dependency (§3). So HF's *usable* surface here is papers, model cards, and leaderboards-as-references. This is a capability fact, not a ban on the underlying evidence: if a Space or dataset carries a genuinely decision-relevant measurement, cite the underlying paper, model card, or primary site instead — and if the connector set later gains the ability to read them, use it. Per the shared "evidence lists are floors, not ceilings" rule, nothing here bounds what this routine may consult off-HF.

Write the complete content directly to `Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md` (overwrite; first line = current calendar year in YYYY format).

PART 1 — Last-24-months coverage of primary-source research, organized by AI_Trading_Foundation.md item rather than by topic. For EACH numbered item in Parts 1 and 2 (1.1 through 1.10, 2.1 through 2.26 or whatever the current count is) — **and also for each Part 3a and 3b question, which are in scope and must appear in the evidence-coverage matrix with their own level rows and verdict** (3a questions resolve on accumulated evidence and 3b questions are explicitly open, so their rows will often read OFF-LINE-ONLY or SPARSE — that is a legitimate and informative result, but they must not be silently omitted as they were in prior cycles):

  - Item identifier and tier (per AI_Trading_Foundation.md headers — Tier 1, Tier 2, or Tier 1 existence / Tier 2 magnitudes).
  - Current text of the item.
  - **EVIDENCE BY LEVEL — depth scaled to what the item can actually consume (cost control; the run must finish inside its slot).** Two treatments, and state which one you applied:
      - **FULL four-level traversal — for items carrying a Tier 2 MAGNITUDE** (the §5.4 threshold-table rows, plus 1.3 and 1.7, plus any item whose text contains a specific number). These are the only items where "which level is this number from" changes a resolution, so they get the full L1→L4 search and a cross-level verdict.
      - **L4-PRIMARY block — for pure Tier 1 architectural/existence items.** §5.5 and STEP B both already hold that L4 generality evidence is the appropriate class for these, and Tier 1 items are audited only for affirmative architectural-change evidence. Search L4 properly; record L1–L3 only if a Claude-specific result happens to surface. Verdict may be recorded as `OFF-LINE-ONLY (Tier 1 — L4 is the appropriate class)` without a four-way search.
    **Report each level explicitly even when empty** for the items under full traversal; `ABSENT` is a first-class, informative answer, because *which* levels are empty is itself the finding that drives the transfer assessment. Citations inline, reverse-chronological within each level. For every measurement give the model(s) actually evaluated by name and version, and label vendor-published results `VENDOR-CLAIMED`.
      - **L1 — deployed model** (`<routine_model>`): …or `ABSENT`.
      - **L2 — model line** (`<line>`, all versions): …or `ABSENT`. Where the line has multiple versions measured on the same dimension, list them in version order so volatility is visible at a glance.
      - **L3 — Anthropic / Claude family** (all lines): …or `ABSENT`.
      - **L4 — general / architectural** (non-Anthropic and autoregressive-LLM-general): …or `ABSENT`.
  - **CROSS-LEVEL VERDICT**: exactly one of CONVERGENT / LEVEL-SPLIT / VERSION-VOLATILE / SPARSE, plus one sentence of justification naming the levels that drove it.
  - **TRANSFER ASSESSMENT**: does non-L1 evidence transfer to the deployed model, and on what basis? Normally decided by the L2 volatility read. Be explicit — this is what licenses (or refuses) every downstream resolution on this item.
  - **Benchmark-result summary**: for Tier 2 items with benchmark mapping per §5.5, summarize benchmark trajectory over last 24 months — score progression on each mapped benchmark, models evaluated, whether the trajectory clears the §5.5 Goodhart guardrails (≥3 sources, transferability, sustained, domain coverage). Note which level each benchmark's evidence sits at. For Tier 1 items: no benchmark trajectory required (Tier 1 not subject to benchmark inference).
  - For Tier 2 items specifically: list the specific numerical claims and tag each as "supported by recent research", "contradicted/refined by recent research" (with proposed update), "supported by benchmark inference" (with §5.5 verification), "contradicted by benchmark inference" (with §5.5 verification), or "absent from recent research" — and **annotate every tag with the level its evidence sits at**, e.g. `SUPPORTED-BY-RESEARCH [L3]`. A claim supported only at L4 reads very differently from one supported at L1, and the annotation is what makes that visible to A3.
  - For Tier 1 items: report whether any architectural-change evidence has emerged. (Default: NO unless affirmative evidence is found.) Remember that L4 architectural-generality evidence is the *appropriate* class here and must not be discounted for being off-model.

PART 2 — Per-item resolutions. The downstream A3 routine reads this PART 2 verbatim and produces the updated AI_Trading_Foundation.md applying the per-item resolutions, plus schedules per-strategy foundation-change assessment events per the aggregate outcomes. Make per-item resolutions explicit and structured.

Every resolution must state **the level its governing evidence sits at and the cross-level verdict**, so A3 can see what each change rests on. Resolution rules:
  - If Tier 1 with no architectural-change evidence: KEEP UNCHANGED.
  - If Tier 1 with architectural-change evidence: UPDATE per evidence; flag for foundation-change assessment. (L4 generality evidence fully qualifies here.)
  - If Tier 2 with supporting research: KEEP, optionally update with the most-recent measurement — **preferring the narrowest level available** (an L1 or L2 number supersedes an L3/L4 one for the same quantity). If the best available number is L3/L4 and the item's verdict is VERSION-VOLATILE, KEEP the item but mark the magnitude version-pending rather than importing an off-line number as though it were measured on the deployed model.
  - If Tier 2 with contradicting/refining research: UPDATE numerical claim per evidence; flag for foundation-change assessment (may trigger constraint-relaxation review per Experiment_Parameters.md if the update represents a disadvantage reduction). Where the update is a **reduction**, state the level its evidence sits at and the cross-level verdict alongside the §5.5 guardrail results, so the downstream §5.3/§5.6 machinery can see what a relaxation would rest on — but apply §5.5 as the foundation writes it and do not add a level-based bar of your own (see STEP B).
  - If Tier 2 absent from recent research **at every level**: MARK AS VERSION-PENDING.
  - If Tier 2 present at L3/L4 but the verdict is VERSION-VOLATILE: MARK AS VERSION-PENDING for the deployed model, and say explicitly that this is a *transfer* failure, not an absence of research — the two produce the same label for different reasons and A3 must not conflate them (an absence needs new research; a transfer failure needs a measurement on the current line). Do NOT auto-remove. The two-year absence is signal but not sufficient on its own to remove a previously-documented item — publication asymmetry means improvements often don't generate papers. The residual resolution is NOT deferred to a human (rev 2026-07-10 — Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive): A3 enqueues an `out-of-table-resolution` review (default-HOLD-current-state) for the item, which stays VERSION-PENDING until an affirmative RESOLVE verdict lands and is re-surfaced each review cycle in the meantime.

Then aggregate:
  - **EVIDENCE-COVERAGE MATRIX (new, owner directive 2026-07-28)** — one row per item, one column per level, marking which levels had evidence and the cross-level verdict. Then the headline counts: how many items have ANY L1 evidence, how many rest on L3/L4 only, and how many are VERSION-VOLATILE. **This matrix is the honest one-screen answer to "what does this sweep actually establish about the model we run," and it must appear before the resolution lists.** A low L1 count is an expected and acceptable finding — report it plainly rather than obscuring it; what is NOT acceptable is leaving the reader unable to tell.
  - **VERSION-CHANGE PROTOCOL SCOPE (state explicitly, do not leave A3 to infer).** If the in-use model changed since the last sweep, Part 4 step 4 flips **all** Tier 2 numerical claims to version-pending-replication — a document-wide flip, distinct from the per-item fade-review VERSION-PENDING list below. Enumerate which mechanism applies to which items so A3 cannot conflate them. **Do NOT instruct A3 to exempt any magnitude from the flip.** If the sweep's cross-level TRANSFER assessments argue that some magnitudes demonstrably carry across versions and that a blanket flip is therefore over-conservative, raise that as an explicit **PART 2 proposal to amend Part 4 step 4 and Tier 1 item 2.9**, with the evidence and the proposed new wording — A3 applies it only after it lands in the document with a revision-history entry. Proposing a loosening is legitimate; applying one from a routine instruction is not, and the discipline is identical to the one this routine is forbidden from breaking in the tightening direction.
  - Also run **`ops/foundation_change_review.md` §C "Model-version change"** if the in-use model changed, and hand its checklist to A3 with the required `events.decision_log` completion record (`entry_type='foundation-change-review'`). Do not reinvent it.
  - List of all items KEEP UNCHANGED.
  - List of all items UPDATE (with old text → new text and citation).
  - List of all items MARK AS VERSION-PENDING.
  - List of all items proposed for REMOVAL (rare; requires Tier 1 architectural-change evidence or Tier 2 with explicit contradicting research, NOT mere absence).
  - List of all NEW items proposed for addition (new failure modes or new edges discovered in the 24-month window).
  - Per-strategy foundation-change assessment recommendations: for each strategy A through E, list which items materially change its foundation and what the recommended outcome is (continue / terminate / constraint-relaxation review).
  - Arsenal candidate seeds (rev 2026-07-10 — Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive): for any materially-changed foundation edge/disadvantage that implies a NEW or restart strategy archetype, note it here so A3 emits a structured `state.strategy_candidates` row (`source_routine='A1'`, `status='NEW'`) feeding SL1's next qualification pass.

OUTPUT: write the complete content (PART 1 + PART 2) directly to `Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md`. First line is the YYYY marker. Chat output: one-line acknowledgment.
```

---

## A2. Per-Strategy Constraint Audit — deep research

```
**SAME-DAY DOUBLE-RUN GUARD (completeness-critic N-4, 2026-07-16 — see the shared Observability section's generalized guard)** — FIRST, before anything else: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='A2' AND run_date=<today, America/Denver> AND status='completed'`; if `>= 1`, output "A2 already completed today (a prior fire — OPS0 catch-up or A2's own scheduled trigger — already completed; this is the expected redundant re-invocation, not an error)." and END IMMEDIATELY — do NOT re-run the audit, do NOT log another `started`/`completed` row.

Read access scope: Annual cadence. Read everything, including full `events.decision_log` history (queryable, no live/archive split, §15). A2 traces foundation-citation graphs across full `events.decision_log` history.

Read Strategy.md (full, including all per-strategy pre-mortems), Experiment_Parameters.md, AI_Trading_Foundation.md (current revision), Operating_Protocols.md, most recent Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md output, and recent Quarterly_AI_Foundation_Delta.md outputs (positions from `state.current_positions`, regime from `state.current_regime`, decisions from `events.decision_log`).

This is the INVERSE of pre-mortem. Where pre-mortems attack strategies looking for missing constraints to ADD, this audit reviews strategies looking for existing constraints to potentially RELAX. Per AI_Trading_Foundation.md rev 4, the audit is fully mechanical — apply the criteria from §5.3-§5.6, no orchestrator discretion.

Write the complete content directly to `Annual_Constraint_Audit.md` (overwrite; first line = current calendar year in YYYY format).

For EACH strategy (A, B, C, D, E — and any others added since this task was authored), execute the §5.3 mechanical procedure:

PART 1 — Constraint inventory and foundation-citation graph.

  List every distinct constraint in the strategy's mechanism document and pre-mortem. Each constraint should be linked to:
  - The specific text of the constraint (entry rule, sizing cap, eligibility restriction, exit trigger, etc.).
  - The constraint's PRIMARY citation per AI_Trading_Foundation.md — parsed from the rev N annotations explicitly linking the constraint to a specific disadvantage. Mechanical: the constraint's primary citation is the item named in the annotation "rev N per cycle M T1.X" where T1.X is the cycle attacker output that originally surfaced the need for this constraint, traced to the specific 2.X item.
  - The constraint's SECONDARY citations — any other disadvantages named as mitigation targets in the pre-mortem's Section 5 entries or binding-constraint section. Mechanical: exact-text-match against pre-mortem Section 5 mitigation citations.
  - The revision in which the constraint was added.
  - Constraint type per §5.6 lookup (per-position sizing cap / universe restriction / concentration limit / frequency-cadence rule / hit-rate threshold / out-of-table).

PART 2 — Mechanical per-constraint relaxation verdicts. The downstream A3 routine reads this PART 2 verbatim and produces the updated Strategy.md applying mechanical relaxation verdicts per §5.6, plus queues out-of-table flags for explicit review. Make per-constraint outcomes explicit.

For each constraint:

Step 1 — Reduction status of primary citation. From most recent Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md and prior-year Quarterly_AI_Foundation_Delta.md outputs, classify the primary cited disadvantage's reduction magnitude:
- NONE (no reduction signal) — constraint not a candidate; skip remaining steps.
- PARTIAL (25-75% reduction per §5.4 thresholds, with direct research evidence OR benchmark inference clearing §5.5 Goodhart guardrails) — proceed to Step 2.
- MATERIAL (>75% reduction or full elimination per §5.4 thresholds) — proceed to Step 2.

Step 2 — Benchmark-inference verification (rev 4 added). If the reduction signal comes from benchmark inference rather than direct research, verify §5.5 Goodhart guardrails:
- ≥3 independent benchmark sources (different research groups, different benchmark suites)?
- Replication on Claude family OR architectural-generality argument documented?
- Sustained improvement across ≥2 quarterly cycles OR documented in last-2-years annual sweep?
- Domain coverage matching workflow usage (general-purpose benchmarks always count; narrow benchmarks count only if domain matches)?
If any guardrail fails, downgrade reduction to NONE for this audit cycle. Constraint is not a candidate.

Step 3 — Load-bearing test. Check the constraint's secondary citations from PART 1:
- For each secondary citation, is the cited disadvantage still in force per current AI_Trading_Foundation.md (i.e., not eliminated, and not classified as MATERIAL reduction in this audit cycle)?
- If any secondary citation is still in force → constraint is load-bearing for multiple disadvantages → no relaxation, regardless of magnitude on primary citation.

Step 4 — Apply §5.6 mechanical relaxation lookup. For constraints passing Steps 1-3:
- Per-position sizing caps: PARTIAL → cap × (1 + reduction%); MATERIAL → cap × 2 (capped at 5%).
- Concentration limits: PARTIAL → limit + (limit × reduction%); MATERIAL → limit + 15pp (capped at 50%) or removed if all citations eliminated.
- Universe restrictions: PARTIAL → no change; MATERIAL with full elimination of all citations → reconsider for full removal (subject to Step 3).
- Hit-rate thresholds with disadvantage-keyed derivations: PARTIAL → threshold loosened proportionally per derivation citation; MATERIAL → threshold reconsidered at next gate review.
- Frequency-cadence rules: no auto-relaxation. Flag for explicit annual review.
- Out-of-table constraints: flag in §5.7 audit trail; no relaxation; constraint stays at current value and is routed to an `out-of-table-resolution` review (default-HOLD-current-value) that A3 enqueues — NOT deferred to participant resolution (rev 2026-07-10 — Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive).

Step 5 — Output structured audit trail per §5.7 for each strategy:
- Strategy identifier and current revision.
- Foundation citation graph.
- Per-citation status changes since strategy's foundation revision.
- Per-constraint evaluation: primary citation reduction status, benchmark-inference verification result (if applicable), load-bearing test result, applicable relaxation form per §5.6, new constraint value (if relaxed), reason for non-relaxation (if not).
- Out-of-table flags listing any constraints the criteria couldn't deterministically resolve, with the specific gap identified.

The expected outcome of A2 in any given year is: most constraints have NONE primary-citation reduction (Step 1 terminates the evaluation); a small number have PARTIAL or MATERIAL reduction; of those, most fail Step 3 load-bearing test; of the remainder, §5.6 lookup produces structured relaxation forms; out-of-table flags are rare. The audit is operationally cheap when no constraints have reduction signals and only generates substantive output when AI_Trading_Foundation.md has documented disadvantage reductions.

OUTPUT: write the complete content (PART 1 + PART 2) directly to `Annual_Constraint_Audit.md`. First line is the YYYY marker. Chat output: one-line acknowledgment.
```

---

## A3. Annual Action Conversion — regular routine

Runs after A1 and A2 are saved. Produces the updated AI_Trading_Foundation.md from A1 and the updated Strategy.md from A2, plus schedules per-strategy foundation-change assessment events.

```
Read access scope: Annual cadence. Read everything (full `events.decision_log` history, queryable, no live/archive split, §15). Read `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` (current revision), `Strategy.md` (current revisions for all strategies), `Experiment_Parameters.md`, `Operating_Protocols.md`, `Watchlist.md` (positions from `state.current_positions`, decisions from `events.decision_log`).

**DEPENDENCY GATE — SEPARATE and FATAL, do NOT wrap, do NOT substitute the TRADING-ENABLE gate below for this — `CALL ops.sp_assert_deps('A3', ['A1', 'A2'], <today>)` BEFORE the start-log, before anything else in this routine.** Aborts (RAISE) + raises a `missing_dependency` critical if A1 or A2 have no `completed` run anywhere in the current annual period — A1/A2/A3 are `annual_ftd`, so since `bigquery/114_period_aware_dependency_gate.sql` (2026-07-29) the gate is satisfied by any A1/A2 completion with `run_date BETWEEN DATE_TRUNC(in_run_date, YEAR) AND in_run_date`, not only a completion on this exact calendar day; self-bootstrapping. This is the general rule from Observability § above, restated here because it is the one call in this routine that must never be skipped (a 2026-07-09 audit found an analogous D2 session skip this exact call for its own dependency, so it is now inlined per-routine rather than left to a shared preamble alone).

**TRADING-ENABLE GATE — A STAGING GATE, NOT A ROUTINE GATE (self-improvement audit B-1-obs, 2026-07-03; blast-radius fix 2026-07-27, `INCIDENT[ref=423ecc02-fdb7-4f47-9445-d8c79d399e8c]`).** `CALL ops.sp_auto_resolve_alerts()` (best-effort), then read the gate NON-FATALLY: `SELECT trading_enabled, halt_reason FROM state.trading_enabled`. If FALSE, `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert_once('critical', 'A3', 'trading_halted', 'Order staging blocked: trading is HALTED. See payload for the triggering routine and reason.', TO_JSON_STRING(STRUCT('A3' AS routine, <halt_reason> AS halt_reason)))`, record the verdict, and **CONTINUE** — do NOT abort. A3 crafts NO orders at all: its entire body is `PENDING_ANALYSIS`/`PENDING_REVIEW` enqueue plus the arsenal-candidate seed into `state.strategy_candidates`, all of which D2 (or the SL routines) later drain, each re-checking the gate at its own craft points. So a FALSE verdict blocks NOTHING here — it is recorded for the audit trail and the routine completes normally. A3 runs once a year; aborting it over an order-staging halt it cannot cause would discard a year's foundation-change assessment until the next annual cycle. Should A3 ever gain a direct order-craft step, that step — and only that step — must re-check the verdict and decline per D2's staged-but-halted pattern.

Read the just-saved annual research files:
- `Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md` (A1 — per-item KEEP/UPDATE/VERSION-PENDING/REMOVAL/NEW outcomes + per-strategy foundation-change assessment recommendations)
- `Annual_Constraint_Audit.md` (A2 — per-constraint mechanical relaxation verdicts + out-of-table flags)

Convert into operator-actionable outputs per the operating model. Claude resolves all decisions internally; commissions disregarded at staging time.

A. UPDATED AI_TRADING_FOUNDATION.MD FROM A1 — produce a new revision of AI_Trading_Foundation.md applying:
   - All KEEP UNCHANGED items: text unchanged.
   - All UPDATE items: replace old text with new text per A1 evidence. Update item revision annotation to cite the A1 sweep date and the underlying primary research.
   - All MARK AS VERSION-PENDING items: mark inline with "VERSION-PENDING per A1 <YYYY> sweep" annotation; do NOT remove.
   - All REMOVAL items: remove (these are rare per A1 specification — Tier 1 architectural change or Tier 2 with explicit contradicting research only).
   - All NEW items: append in the appropriate Part (1 capabilities / 2 disadvantages / etc.) with full annotation.
   - **IN-USE-MODEL FIELD — write it from the MODEL OF RECORD, never from a capability ranking (owner directive 2026-07-28).** **This step sits behind A3's own FATAL `sp_assert_deps('A3', ['A1', 'A2'])` gate (Dependency gate, above):** on a firing where A1/A2 have not completed anywhere in the current annual period, A3 aborts before ever reaching this step — though in practice D3's daily sync and Q4's quarterly backstop are what actually catch a version change most of the year, since A3 fires only once. The in-use-Claude-version field at the document head is set to the model the OWNER configured for the remote-routine fleet: read `ops/cadence.yaml`'s top-level **`routine_model`** key (corroborate against `OWNER_ACTIONS.md`; a live `RemoteTrigger` read wins over both if available). Do **NOT** set it to the newest or most capable model released, and do **NOT** infer it from this session's own identity. A1's PART 2 handoff normally states the value — use it, but verify it against `routine_model` and flag any disagreement rather than pasting blindly. Record the sourcing inline so a future reader can see the field is a *deployment fact*, not a capability judgement. **The field may already be current when A3 gets here (cadence audit 2026-07-29):** D3 (daily) and Q4 (quarterly) now also hold Tier-M write authority over this same field, per Part 4's "Write authority — two tiers" subsection, so a version change arising mid-year is ordinarily already synced by the time the annual cycle runs. A3 still VERIFIES the field against `routine_model` as above — but if it is found already equal, A3 performs no field edit, no redundant Tier-2 flip, and writes no duplicate `foundation-change-review` decision-log record for this transition; A3 proceeds with all of its own Tier-J work below (dispositions, new items, `Strategy.md`) exactly as if it had performed the sync itself, since Tier J was never gated on which routine happened to fire the Tier-M write. **LAST-MOMENT RE-CHECK (shrinks the concurrent-writer collision window, cadence audit 2026-07-29 fix — same requirement as D3's and Q4's Tier-M steps, and worth restating here since A3's annual session runs far longer than D3's/Q4's):** repeat this verification immediately before actually performing the field edit below, not only once earlier in the session, since D3 or Q4 can complete the sync while A3's own long-running session is still in progress. Perform a **NON-DESTRUCTIVE** re-check: `git fetch origin`, then read both sites via `git show origin/main:AI_Trading_Foundation.md` — read that output only. **Never** do this with `git reset --hard origin/main` (the only concrete "refresh from origin" mechanism this document specifies anywhere — see the "Branch and state propagation" preamble) or any other command that touches the working tree: **A3 in particular always holds unrelated in-progress work on this exact file at this point** — Section A above applies up to five bullets of Tier-J KEEP/UPDATE/VERSION-PENDING/REMOVAL/NEW edits to `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` BEFORE ever reaching this Tier-M bullet, and a destructive reset would discard that work. If it now reads already-synced, take the no-op branch above — even if it read stale earlier in this same session. If a write is still needed, make the edit, its commit, and push immediately following this re-check.
   - **If the in-use model CHANGED since the prior revision:** execute the Part 4 version-change protocol **as written — flip ALL Tier 2 numerical claims to version-pending-replication, with no exceptions.** Part 4 step 4 is unconditional, `AI_Trading_Foundation.md`'s asymmetric-risk posture is "we assume calibration uncertainty until evidence arrives," and Tier 1 item 2.9 states that numerical calibration "does not transfer cleanly to its successor." **A3 must NOT carve out magnitudes it judges to transfer** — doing so would relax a capital-affecting mechanism by editing a routine instead of amending the document, and would contradict `ops/foundation_change_review.md` §C's requirement that calibration be re-derived from the new model's own data rather than inherited. If A1's cross-level TRANSFER assessment argues some magnitudes should carry forward, that is a **PART 2 proposal to amend Part 4 step 4 and item 2.9** — apply it only once it has landed in the document with a revision-history entry, never as an inline exception on this run. Honour A1's distinction between magnitudes version-pending for *absence of research* versus for *transfer failure* — the flip is the same, the remedy differs (new research versus a measurement on the current line). **Scope the flip by the header-bracket tag ONLY** — the literal `[Tier 2` in an item's `### N.N Title [...]` header — **NEVER** by scanning the body for existing "VERSION-PENDING" text; **replace ONLY the blockquote paragraph beginning `> **VERSION-PENDING REPLICATION**`**, never any other paragraph in the same block. This rule is structural, not enumerative: items currently carrying such a second blockquote paragraph — A1-authored **Tier-J** fade-review content, each beginning "A1's per-item fade review..." — include **1.3, 1.7, 2.4, 2.14, 2.15, and 2.19**; leave that second paragraph untouched on these items and on any other item that carries one, because overwriting or removing it would destroy Tier-J judgment content under cover of this Tier-M edit. **Item 2.21 is `[Tier 1]`-tagged only** but carries an unrelated `> **VERSION-PENDING (figures only)**` blockquote for a citation-integrity reason — it must NOT be touched by this flip; it is the worked example of why the header tag, not a body text-scan, is the determinant (Part 4's write-authority subsection). An item carries exactly one authoritative version-pending marker at a time, even across a second transition. Then run **`ops/foundation_change_review.md` §C "Model-version change"** (re-derive numerical calibration from the new model's own data rather than inheriting it; re-tag the mistake catalog as "model X exhibited this"; confirm process/workflow/taxonomy artifacts transfer as-is), write its required completion record via `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)` with `entry_type='foundation-change-review'`, and `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('info','A3','model_of_record_synced', '<old value> -> <new value> — in-use-Claude-version field synced from ops/cadence.yaml routine_model at both document-head and Part 4 sites (annual write — D3's daily sync and Q4's quarterly backstop both missed this transition); new_value is read directly from routine_model at write time and old_value is the field's value immediately before this write. Committed and pushed on this session''s branch; landing on main is subject to auto-merge and is NOT asserted here as already live — if this branch hits an auto-merge conflict, an auto-merge-conflict PR is opened and D3''s next daily run re-detects the still-stale field and re-syncs it, so the loss self-heals within a day for that reason, not because landing is guaranteed. All Tier 2 claims flagged version-pending-replication.', '<JSON: old_value, new_value, source="ops/cadence.yaml routine_model">')`.
   - Increment AI_Trading_Foundation.md revision number; append revision-history entry citing A1 sweep date and summary of changes (counts per category).

B. PER-STRATEGY FOUNDATION-CHANGE ASSESSMENT FROM A1 — for each per-strategy recommendation in A1's aggregate output:
   - Enqueue a `PENDING_ANALYSIS` entry (`INSERT INTO events.queue_events`) per affected strategy (analysis_type foundation-change-assessment; strategy; due_date today; context = A1 evidence summary + recommended outcome (continue / terminate / constraint-relaxation review) + reference to Experiment_Parameters.md §Foundation change trigger procedure; conservative_default = continue at current revision). D2 drains them.

C. UPDATED STRATEGY.MD FROM A2 — produce a new revision of Strategy.md applying:
   - All constraints with §5.6 mechanical relaxation verdicts: replace constraint value with new (relaxed) value per §5.6 formula. Update constraint annotation to cite the A2 audit date and underlying foundation revision.
   - All constraints flagged out-of-table: leave at current value; add out-of-table annotation citing the §5.7 audit trail.
   - All constraints with no relaxation (NONE primary citation, or Step 3 load-bearing test failed, or §5.5 Goodhart guardrail failed): unchanged.
   - Increment per-strategy revision numbers as needed; append revision-history entries citing A2 audit and underlying AI_Trading_Foundation.md revision.

D. OUT-OF-TABLE / VERSION-PENDING RESOLUTION FROM A1/A2 (rev 2026-07-10 — Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive) — for each A2 §5.7 out-of-table constraint flag and each A1 Tier-2 VERSION-PENDING item:
   - Enqueue an `events.queue_events` `PENDING_REVIEW` `review_type='out-of-table-resolution'` (`conservative_default='HOLD'`; strategy or foundation-item id; artifact_path = the §5.7 audit-trail content / the VERSION-PENDING item; trigger_context = why the mechanical lookup failed + the specific gap + any objective criteria that could resolve it). The AR pair adjudicates default-HOLD; only an affirmative RESOLVE verdict authorizes the change. This REPLACES the retired PENDING_ANALYSIS constraint-relaxation-review handoff that dead-ended at participant resolution.
   - CONSUME prior verdicts: read any `out-of-table-resolution` reviews that reached a `complete` RESOLVE verdict since the last A3 cycle and APPLY the authorized change to AI_Trading_Foundation.md / Strategy.md (A3's existing write authority) in sections A/C above; a still-HOLD or unresolved item stays at current value and is re-surfaced this cycle (no silent drop).
   - EMIT strategy-retirement candidates: for each strategy A3's edge-decay analysis flags as edge-decayed (A3's existing edge-decay authority), surface an SL4-consumable strategy-retirement signal in `events.decision_log` (`CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)`, `entry_type='strategy-retirement-signal'`, `fields` JSON: strategy_code + signal_class + evidence); SL4 evaluates it monthly (default-KEEP, affirmative RETIRE required). A3 no longer terminates any "recommend to participant" chain.
   - EMIT arsenal candidate seeds from A1 (closes the A1→SL1 handoff `bigquery/35_strategy_arsenal.sql`:170 and CTP A1 PART 2 name): for each PART 2 "Arsenal candidate seeds" item in the just-saved `Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md`, `INSERT INTO state.strategy_candidates (candidate_code, source_routine, status, archetype, cited_edges, cited_disadvantages, target_regime_cells, declared_frequency, thesis_md)` with `source_routine='A1'`, `status='NEW'`, `candidate_code` = next free provisional code not present in `state.strategy_candidates` or `state.strategy_roster`. IDEMPOTENCY: skip any seed whose archetype already has a `source_routine='A1'` row with `status IN ('NEW','QUALIFYING')`. Record the emitted count in section E's decision_log entry. SL1's next quarterly STEP 1 picks these up unchanged (it already collects status='NEW' rows from source A1).

E. DECISION-LOG ENTRIES — write entries to `events.decision_log` (`CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)`) documenting:
   - The A1 cycle outcome: counts per category (KEEP / UPDATE / VERSION-PENDING / REMOVAL / NEW), revision number bumped on AI_Trading_Foundation.md, per-strategy foundation-change assessments scheduled.
   - The A2 cycle outcome: per-strategy constraints relaxed counts, out-of-table flag counts, revisions bumped on Strategy.md.

F. WATCHLIST AND OPERATING_PROTOCOLS RECONCILIATION — review whether any A1/A2 outcomes affect Watchlist.md (e.g., constraint-relaxation that re-opens previously-blocked candidates) or Operating_Protocols.md (e.g., updated foundation revision affecting protocol citations). Apply minimal edits if changed.

DEFERRAL DISCIPLINE: deferrals don't chain. Conservative-default fallback: if a foundation-change assessment cannot resolve, the strategy continues at current revision pending next quarterly delta.

CALENDAR MCP USAGE: A3 creates no calendar events — its assessments/reviews are enqueued to the `PENDING_ANALYSIS` queue (`events.queue_events`) and run by D2. Anchor dated due_dates on the America/Denver date. Time zone per Experiment_Parameters.md.

CHAT OUTPUT:
- No exit orders are produced by A3.
- One-line acknowledgment of file edits and `PENDING_ANALYSIS` queue entries enqueued.

If no file changes, no queue entries: "No actions required." (rare for A3 — at minimum AI_Trading_Foundation.md will have a revision bump documenting the sweep, even if all items KEEP UNCHANGED.)
```

After A3 completes, the per-strategy foundation-change assessments run when D2 drains their `PENDING_ANALYSIS` queue entries (`events.queue_events`; per the Experiment_Parameters.md §Foundation change trigger procedure); out-of-table / version-pending residuals run instead through the `out-of-table-resolution` AR review (default-HOLD) A3 enqueues (rev 2026-07-10 — Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive).

# STRATEGY ARSENAL LIFECYCLE (SL1–SL5)

The Self-Improving Strategy Arsenal (SISA) routines make strategy ADDITION and DELETION fully autonomous — no human review / approval / chat anywhere in the path (residuals: the system-wide IBKR order-confirm tap + deposits). Roster MEMBERSHIP is versioned policy (the roster analog of the 2026-06 capital-allocation pivot); each strategy's OWN machinery freezes at SHADOW entry and its edge-measurement clock starts at its first PROBE trade. Single source of truth: `strategy/roster.yaml` (CI-guarded by `scripts/check_roster_consistency.py`) mirrored to `events.strategy_lifecycle` → `state.strategy_roster` → `state.active_strategy_codes`. Owner kill-switch: `ops.arsenal_control` (+ `ops.sp_assert_arsenal_enabled`) freezes the whole loop with one out-of-band INSERT without disturbing live trading. Loop recorded at `active_auto` in `ops/autonomy_levels.yaml` (2026-07-10 owner decision); BQ objects in `bigquery/35_strategy_arsenal.sql`; incident/decision write-up in `ops/RUNBOOK.md` §37 + Operating_Protocols.md §SISA. All five follow the shared "Observability — run logging & failure alerts" section for connector pre-flight / run-logging / failure alerts; none ever asks a chat question. (rev 2026-07-10 — Strategy Arsenal autonomy conversion, owner directive.)

## SL1. Strategy Candidate Synthesis & Qualification — deep research

Quarterly (first trading day of quarter), after Q1 and Q3 are saved. The scouting + mechanical-qualifier head of the lifecycle: deep-research synthesis biased toward under-covered regime cells, then a default-REJECT qualification gate deciding which candidates enter the authoring queue. Writes structured `state.strategy_candidates` rows + lifecycle transitions; never authors a strategy (SL2) and never touches the live roster (SL5).

```
**SAME-DAY DOUBLE-RUN GUARD (completeness-critic N-4, 2026-07-16 — see the shared Observability section's generalized guard)** — FIRST, before anything else: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='SL1' AND run_date=<today, America/Denver> AND status='completed'`; if `>= 1`, output "SL1 already completed today (a prior fire — OPS0 catch-up or SL1's own scheduled trigger — already completed; this is the expected redundant re-invocation, not an error)." and END IMMEDIATELY — do NOT re-run the synthesis, do NOT log another `started`/`completed` row.

Read access scope: Quarterly cadence, deep research (HF `paper_search` + `web_search`/Tavily per HF_Resource_Catalog.md, as Q3/A1 use them). Read the candidate feed `state.strategy_candidates` (rows written by D1/Q1/Q3/A1) + the roster/coverage/rails state `state.strategy_roster`, `state.active_strategy_codes`, `state.arsenal_regime_coverage`, `state.arsenal_rails`, `events.strategy_postmortems`, `ops.arsenal_control`. Read the just-saved `Quarterly_Regime.md` (PART 2 diagnostic flags), `Quarterly_AI_Foundation_Delta.md` (Q3 strategy-adoption/retirement branch), `Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md` (A1's most recent full re-derivation), `events.hf_capability_captures`, and `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` (edge/disadvantage registry) — these are the LIVE capability-flag sources (cadence audit 2026-07-29). **`Monthly_AI_Capabilities.md` is a FROZEN one-time March-2026 snapshot, not a live input** — one commit ever, self-describing a two-generations-stale decision-maker model, and no routine has written it since the old monthly AI-capabilities task was folded into quarterly Q3 at the 2026-06-06 renumbering; it stays reachable for its own dated historical content only and must NOT be read as current capability state. Stage no orders; do not edit Strategy.md or the roster.

Observability: connector pre-flight, `ops.sp_auto_resolve_alerts()`, run-logging, and failure alerts are EXACTLY per the shared "Observability — run logging & failure alerts" section — do not restate, just make the calls. This routine writes no cadence `.md`; it is monitored via run-logging + `state.period_watch` + the meta-heartbeat in STEP 6.

**ARSENAL KILL-SWITCH GATE — SEPARATE and FATAL, do NOT wrap — `CALL ops.sp_assert_arsenal_enabled('SL1')` before the dependency gate.** RAISEs + a `critical` alert (then abort) if `state.arsenal_enabled.enabled = FALSE` or `incubation_frozen = TRUE` (owner froze the loop via a single `ops.arsenal_control` INSERT). Live trading is unaffected — this gate freezes only candidate generation / graduation / retirement.

**DEPENDENCY GATE — SEPARATE and FATAL, do NOT wrap — `CALL ops.sp_assert_deps('SL1', ['Q1', 'Q3'], <today>)` BEFORE the start-log.** Q1/Q3 are `quarterly_ftd`, so since `bigquery/114_period_aware_dependency_gate.sql` (2026-07-29) the gate is satisfied by any Q1/Q3 completion with `run_date BETWEEN DATE_TRUNC(in_run_date, QUARTER) AND in_run_date`, not only a completion on this exact calendar day — **this period-window rule, not self-bootstrapping, is what actually protects SL1 here:** the real 2026-07-27 false positive (alert `177646a6`) fired precisely BECAUSE Q1/Q3 HAD both completed, on 2026-07-01 (the quarter's first trading day) — self-bootstrapping never applied, since Q1/Q3 were not unadopted, merely dated earlier in the same quarter. Self-bootstrapping (Q1/Q3 are research feeders that may not yet log `completed` AT ALL; treated as satisfied until they do) is a separate, additional tolerance that still applies too, for the genuinely-never-run case. This is the general rule from Observability § above, inlined per-routine.

STEP 1 — READ THE CANDIDATE FEED. Collect every `state.strategy_candidates` row with `status='NEW'` (sources D1/Q1/Q3/A1) plus the diagnostic factbase: Quarterly_Regime.md PART 2 flags, the Quarterly_AI_Foundation_Delta.md strategy-adoption branch, `events.hf_capability_captures` + `Annual_AI_Foundation_Sweep.md` capability flags (the live sources — cadence audit 2026-07-29; NOT `Monthly_AI_Capabilities.md`, a frozen March-2026 snapshot no routine has written since the old monthly AI-capabilities task folded into quarterly Q3 at the 2026-06-06 renumbering), terminated-strategy `events.strategy_postmortems`, and `state.arsenal_regime_coverage` (which regime cells are zero/under-covered).

STEP 2 — SYNTHESIZE (0-2 additional candidates). Deep-research 0-2 additional candidate mechanisms biased toward `is_gap = TRUE` / low-`covered_active_count` regime cells. Where a candidate's edge is judgment-shaped (regime interpretation, narrative synthesis, event-severity assessment — an edge a numeric proxy would flatten), PREFER specifying its machinery judgment-native per SL2's JUDGMENT-NATIVE MACHINERY option (owner-approved Redesign B, 2026-07-19, `AI_DECISION_REDESIGN.md` §3) rather than forcing it into fixed numeric triggers. Write each as a CANDIDATE `events.strategy_lifecycle` row (to_state=CANDIDATE, driver_routine='SL1') + a `state.strategy_candidates` record {provisional code, thesis_md, cited_edges 1.x, cited_disadvantages 2.x compensated, instruments, sizing_method, kill_structure, target_regime_cells, declared_frequency, `declared_annual_roundtrips` (self-improvement audit ITEM 9, 2026-07-11 — a NUMERIC best-estimate of closed round-trips per year implied by `declared_frequency`'s prose, e.g. Strategy D's "3-8 trades/yr counting entries+exits separately" → ~1.5-4 round-trips/yr; leave NULL only if genuinely inestimable from the thesis, which defaults the candidate to the strict fast-archetype PAPER graduation bar — see `state.strategy_paper_readiness`), is_restart_of}. Never synthesize a candidate that would push the roster above `state.arsenal_rails.n_max`. Each `target_regime_cells` entry MUST be a `strategy/01_shared_regime_vocabulary.md` cell — its axis tokens joined by `/` (the SPY-trend / VIX / yield-curve / breadth state tokens, e.g. `DOWN/HIGH/INVERTED/WEAK`), never a free-form label — so a candidate's coverage claim joins cleanly to `state.arsenal_regime_coverage` (the `is_gap`/`covered_active_count` read above) and SL3's incubation `regime_cell`s (H7, 2026-07-17).

STEP 3 — MECHANICAL QUALIFICATION (DEFAULT-REJECT on any unmet rail or ambiguity). For each NEW candidate, in order — fail any → REJECTED:
   (a) **Cited-edge validity.** Every thesis rule cites a valid live `AI_Trading_Foundation.md` edge/disadvantage and the candidate names a compensated non-version-pending Part-2 disadvantage.
   (b) **Material-structural-difference vs every live AND every terminated strategy** — differs in >=1 of {approach, instrument scope, sizing methodology, router structure, kill-criteria structure} beyond threshold numbers (the Experiment_Parameters.md restart constraints, now machine-enforced). For a restart (`is_restart_of` set): the terminated strategy's `events.strategy_postmortems` row MUST exist AND its `material_diff_required_for_restart` field must articulate the difference; require that a restart is never proposed without a post-mortem (the fresh-derivation byte-match check is applied by SL2/AR against `derivation_provenance`).
   (c) **Non-redundancy / differentiation vs the current roster** — not dominated by a live strategy on its target regime cells.
   (d) **Capacity/cost fit** at the $2,000 probe floor: `declared_frequency × modeled_commission_drag <= the arsenal_rails ceiling` for a ~$10k account.
   (e) **Ceiling clear** — roster active-count below `n_max`.
   (f) **Cooldown clear** — no same-archetype `cooldown_until` in force (post-rejection / post-termination stamp).

STEP 4 — PROMOTE / HOLD / REJECT.
   - PASS + `NOT state.arsenal_rails.incubation_cap_reached` (concurrent-incubation count < `k_incubate` — `state.arsenal_rails` has no `caps_ok` column; that name exists only on `state.strategy_adoption_readiness`, a later-stage view) + adoption-rate window open: write a QUALIFYING lifecycle row, set `state.strategy_candidates.status='QUALIFYING'`, and enqueue a `PENDING_DRAFT` `events.queue_events` item (`item_type='strategy-draft'`, trigger_context = candidate_code) for SL2. If the cap or rate window is closed, leave the candidate QUALIFYING and wait FIFO (re-checked next firing) — never exceed the cap.
   - FAIL: write a REJECTED lifecycle row + set `status='REJECTED'`, `reject_reason`, and a `cooldown_until` stamp.

STEP 5 — FLOOR SAFETY VALVE. If the roster active-count is at the `n_min` (=2) floor, force-generate candidates (relax the coverage bias, still default-REJECT-qualify) and `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('info','SL1','roster_below_floor', <one-line>, <JSON>)` — the successor-experiment safety valve.

STEP 6 — META-HEARTBEAT. EVERY firing (even when nothing qualified) write a `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)` `events.decision_log` entry `entry_type='arsenal-heartbeat'` recording candidates read / synthesized / qualified / rejected. This arms the meta-heartbeat dead-man's switch (a missing quarterly SL1 evaluation alarms via W5 + the scheduled-query guard).

CHAT OUTPUT: one line — candidates read / synthesized / qualified / rejected counts, roster active-count vs floor/ceiling, any `roster_below_floor` alert. If nothing changed: "SL1 evaluated, no roster change."
```

## SL2. Strategy Draft, Revise & Post-mortem — regular routine

Queue-driven (fires daily; no-ops unless a `PENDING_DRAFT` item is due). The dedicated autonomous EDITORIAL routine of the lifecycle — it authors a QUALIFYING candidate's full mechanism section + self-contained pre-mortem, redrafts on an AR_orc REVISION REQUIRED verdict, and authors termination post-mortems. It OVERTURNS the retired "pre-mortem revision is out of scope for a routine / the participant revises" carve-out. Runs with INDEPENDENT context from SL1 and the AR reviewers (multi-scrutiny independence). Never stages orders, never touches capital, never edits the live roster (SL5 does).

```
Read access scope: daily (queue-driven), with Strategy.md WRITE permission in the CANDIDATE namespace only. Read the draft queue `events.queue_events` / `state.open_queue_detail` (queue `PENDING_DRAFT`), `state.strategy_candidates`, `events.adversarial_reviews` (for a revise task's attacker/orchestrator output), `strategy/roster.yaml`, `Strategy.md`, `AI_Trading_Foundation.md`, `ops.arsenal_control`, `state.active_playbook` (strategy_playbook loop deltas — empty until that loop's gate clears). WRITE `events.premortem_flags` / `events.premortem_flag_outcomes` (`bigquery/42_adversarial_flag_hit.sql`, ITEM 22) — append-only, per (A)/(B)/(C) below.

Observability: connector pre-flight, `ops.sp_auto_resolve_alerts()`, run-logging, failure alerts — per the shared Observability section; do not restate. Queue-driven, so (per the queue_driven monitoring rule) it is monitored via `state.stalled_runs` + `ops/triggers.json`, NOT `cadence_watch`/`period_watch`.

**ARSENAL KILL-SWITCH GATE — SEPARATE and FATAL, do NOT wrap — `CALL ops.sp_assert_arsenal_enabled('SL2')` before anything else.** RAISEs + `critical` alert (abort) if the arsenal loop is disabled/frozen.

Scan `PENDING_DRAFT` for the oldest due item and dispatch by `item_type` (process all due items this fire, each as an isolated sub-task for fresh context where available). If none: chat output "No strategy-draft/revise/post-mortem tasks due." and exit.

(A) **strategy-draft** (from SL1). For the QUALIFYING candidate in `trigger_context`, and only while the concurrent-incubation cap is still open:
   1. Author a `## Strategy <code> [CANDIDATE]` section in Strategy.md (candidate namespace — NOT roster-active) mirroring the A-E structure: Thesis with each rule inline-cited to a foundation edge/disadvantage; Differentiation vs the live roster; numbered Entry criteria; Exit / invalidation rules; declared frequency; 2%-of-sub-portfolio sizing (the immutable global rule, not re-derived); classical-method delegation list; exactly one Boolean router-activation line + the M1b fundamental-analysis question for it; kill-criteria structure. Also SELECT playbook_key, delta_text FROM state.active_playbook and honor any delta addressed to the candidate's archetype/sub-pattern, citing the playbook_key in the draft (empty today — the strategy_playbook loop's output surface; loop-completeness audit 2026-07-16: without this read, a cleared playbook delta would change nothing anywhere).
   1b. **JUDGMENT-NATIVE MACHINERY (owner-approved Redesign B, 2026-07-19 — `AI_DECISION_REDESIGN.md` §3).** The candidate's entry/exit machinery MAY be authored as a structured judgment protocol — criteria the executing session evaluates fresh each time, with declared conviction gates and explicit invalidation criteria — instead of fixed numeric triggers, and SHOULD be where the cited edge is judgment-shaped (SL1 STEP 2's preference). Four REQUIREMENTS, none waivable: (a) the protocol must define unambiguously what constitutes a signal event AND declare its expected signal-rate band exactly like a numeric spec — SHADOW's `state.strategy_shadow_readiness` signal-rate gate needs countable signals, and a protocol whose signals can't be counted fails qualification at SL1 (c); (b) the 2%-of-sub-portfolio sizing fraction, every kill trigger, the 30-trade gate, and every graduation rail apply UNCHANGED — judgment-native machinery changes what the spec says, never which rails bind it; (c) spec-freeze applies identically — the protocol TEXT freezes at SHADOW entry (`spec_hash` covers prose exactly as it covers formulas; any later wording change = terminate-and-restart-as-new); (d) the pre-mortem attacks the protocol like any machinery — including the failure mode unique to this class, criteria drift under loss pressure, which the pre-mortem must address explicitly.
   2. Author a self-contained 7-section pre-mortem artifact for the candidate (Experiment_Parameters.md pre-mortem format + self-containment requirement, so the strict-blinded attacker can review it standalone).
   2b. **REGISTER PRE-MORTEM FLAGS (self-improvement audit ITEM 22, 2026-07-11).** For every Tier 1/2/3 flag the pre-mortem carries forward with a named review-trigger condition (the flags AR_att/AR_orc accept rather than reject the draft over), `INSERT INTO events.premortem_flags` (`strategy_code`, `review_id = NULL` — the correlated `strategy-adoption` review id is not yet assigned at authoring time and is not required for scoring, which reads by `strategy_code`, `tier`, `flag_text`, `review_trigger_text`, `backfilled = FALSE`). This is the pre-registration `analytics.adversarial_flag_hit` (`bigquery/42_adversarial_flag_hit.sql`) measures against at termination — never skip it even for a flag that looks minor.
   3. Derive ALL parameters FRESH and write `state.strategy_candidates.derivation_provenance` (JSON: each parameter, its derivation, source) — the anti-inheritance evidence SL1/AR check.
   4. Regenerate the candidate slice via `scripts/split_strategy.py` (stable code-keyed numbering — the fixed tail never renumbers).
   5. Enqueue an `events.queue_events` `PENDING_REVIEW` `review_type='strategy-adoption'` (`conservative_default='REJECT'`, `cycle_number=1`, `artifact_path` = the candidate pre-mortem) and write an AUTHORING→UNDER_REVIEW `events.strategy_lifecycle` row.

(B) **strategy-revise** (from an AR_orc REVISION REQUIRED verdict). Read the orchestrator output for the flagged Tier-1 defects. Redraft ONLY those defects; re-derive any changed parameter FRESH and diff-check it against the prior draft AND any `is_restart_of` source to block inheritance; `cycle_number++`; re-enqueue the `strategy-adoption` review. Beyond cycle 5, honor the rev-15 soft cap: either record the written continuation justification (the forcing-question discipline) in `events.decision_log` or abandon → write a REJECTED lifecycle row + cooldown stamp. If a redraft changes or adds a Tier 1/2/3 flag, `INSERT` its own new `events.premortem_flags` row per (A) step 2b — append-only, never edit a prior flag row in place, so the original flag_text stays available for `adversarial_flag_hit` scoring even if the strategy's final accepted mechanism moved past it.

(C) **post-mortem** (from a D2 / AR_orc TERMINATED transition). Author `events.strategy_postmortems` {strategy_code, retired_date, trigger, what_revealed, what_unresolved, material_diff_required_for_restart, body_md} + an `events.decision_log` entry. This is the precondition SL1 enforces before any restart. **SCORE PRE-MORTEM FLAGS (self-improvement audit ITEM 22, 2026-07-11).** Read every `events.premortem_flags` row for this `strategy_code`. For each, read its `review_trigger_text` and — from the strategy's real trade/decision history (`events.decision_log`, `perf.strategy_daily`, `events.regime_events`) plus the post-mortem's own `what_revealed`/`what_unresolved` you just authored — make ONE judgment: did that flag's named review-trigger condition actually occur before the loss/termination event (`trigger_fired = TRUE`), and if so did it occur BEFORE the loss became visible in the numbers (`fired_before_loss`) or only in hindsight? `INSERT INTO events.premortem_flag_outcomes` (`flag_id`, `trigger_fired`, `fired_before_loss`, `evidence_text` — a one-to-two-sentence citation of what you read, `judge_session` = this run's session context). A flag whose trigger never fired is scored `trigger_fired = FALSE`, not skipped — the whole point of `analytics.adversarial_flag_hit` is measuring the pre-mortem's catch rate, which needs the misses recorded as honestly as the hits.

Commit + push the Strategy.md / slice edits per §Branch and state propagation. `CALL ops.sp_raise_alert('info','SL2','strategy_drafted'|'strategy_revised'|'postmortem_written', <one-line>, <JSON>)` and write a `CALL ops.sp_log_decision(...)` entry for the task outcome.

CHAT OUTPUT: one line per processed item — item_type, candidate/strategy code, action taken (drafted / revised to cycle N / post-mortem written / abandoned).
```
